### DeepShade: Enable Shade Simulation by Text-conditioned Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12103v1)
- **Authors**: Longchao Da, Xiangrui Liu, Mithun Shivakoti, Thirulogasankar Pranav Kutralingam, Yezhou Yang, Hua Wei
- **Abstract**: Heatwaves pose a significant threat to public health, especially as global warming intensifies. However, current routing systems (e.g., online maps) fail to incorporate shade information due to the difficulty of estimating shades directly from noisy satellite imagery and the limited availability of training data for generative models. In this paper, we address these challenges through two main contributions. First, we build an extensive dataset covering diverse longitude-latitude regions, varying levels of building density, and different urban layouts. Leveraging Blender-based 3D simulations alongside building outlines, we capture building shadows under various solar zenith angles throughout the year and at different times of day. These simulated shadows are aligned with satellite images, providing a rich resource for learning shade patterns. Second, we propose the DeepShade, a diffusion-based model designed to learn and synthesize shade variations over time. It emphasizes the nuance of edge features by jointly considering RGB with the Canny edge layer, and incorporates contrastive learning to capture the temporal change rules of shade. Then, by conditioning on textual descriptions of known conditions (e.g., time of day, solar angles), our framework provides improved performance in generating shade images. We demonstrate the utility of our approach by using our shade predictions to calculate shade ratios for real-world route planning in Tempe, Arizona. We believe this work will benefit society by providing a reference for urban planning in extreme heat weather and its potential practical applications in the environment.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper "DeepShade: Enable Shade Simulation by Text-conditioned Image Generation." Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of accurately simulating shade patterns in urban environments for applications like heat-aware route planning.  The authors argue that current methods suffer from limitations in data availability, scalability, and real-time adaptability. To overcome these, they introduce two main contributions:  (1) a novel, globally representative dataset comprising satellite images, 3D building models (generated using Blender based on OSM data), and corresponding shade simulations under varying solar conditions, (2) a diffusion-based model called DeepShade. DeepShade leverages edge conditioning (Canny edge detection integrated with RGB data) and contrastive learning to generate realistic and temporally consistent shade images, conditioned on text descriptions (time of day, solar angle). The paper showcases the model's performance across different cities, ablates its components, and demonstrates its utility in a shade-aware route planning application.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a combination of contributions that, taken together, offer reasonable novelty.
    *   The dataset is a significant contribution.  The creation of a large-scale, geographically diverse dataset of aligned satellite imagery, 3D building models, and shade simulations fills a gap in the existing research. The meticulous attention to aligning data across modalities is a plus.  Although urban scene understanding has been studied by [Da et al., 2025b], urban environments remain limited [Park et al., 2023].
    *   The DeepShade model builds on existing diffusion models like ControlNet but incorporates two key modifications: edge conditioning and contrastive learning. Edge conditioning helps generate accurate shade boundaries, addressing a clear limitation of vanilla diffusion models in capturing fine details. The contrastive learning component, enforced by the use of contrastive buffers, promotes temporal consistency in shade generation, which is especially useful for enabling dynamic scene simulation.

*   **Significance:** The work has the potential to make a real-world impact on urban planning and public health.
    *   The shade-aware route planning application demonstrates the practical utility of the generated shade data.  By integrating shade information into route optimization, it helps individuals reduce heat exposure, addressing a pressing concern in increasingly hot urban environments.
    *   The scalability of the approach, due to the use of readily available satellite imagery and OSM data, is a key strength. This makes it potentially applicable to diverse cities and regions, unlike methods that rely on resource-intensive LiDAR data.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem, limitations of existing methods, and the motivations behind the proposed approach.
    *   The technical details are explained clearly. The description of the dataset generation process and the DeepShade model architecture are well-structured.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive, demonstrating the effectiveness of the model across different cities, ablating the components, and showcasing a practical application.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The main limitations of the model is that if the dataset containing ground-truth shades in the area of study is biased, then the model may not perform in real-time shade planning. As the current OSM data is prone to be updated and is usually behind, thus, it will require regular dataset refresh and ground-truth validation.
    *   The reliance on OpenStreetMap (OSM) data for building outlines is a potential limitation.  The accuracy and completeness of OSM data vary significantly across regions, which could affect the quality of the generated shade simulations.
    *   While the experimental results are promising, further validation of the shade predictions against real-world shade measurements (e.g., using sensors or crowdsourced data) would strengthen the findings.
    *   The ablation study could be more detailed in terms of varying parameters. The ablations are limited to removing parts of the model, without varying hyperparameters, which does not provide the depth of knowledge necessary to show their robustness.

*   **Impact:**
    *   The work has the potential to influence urban planning practices by providing a cost-effective and scalable way to simulate shade patterns.
    *   It can contribute to the development of personalized route planning applications that prioritize heat safety.
    *   The dataset and model could serve as a valuable resource for other researchers working on urban scene understanding and environmental simulation.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7/10**.

*   The strengths are in its novelty, significance, and clear presentation. The novel integration of data and the focus on a significant problem (heat mitigation) is an advantage.
*   The weaknesses are the dependence on imperfect OSM data, limitations in validation.

The work represents a solid contribution with tangible practical potential. Addressing these points could substantially elevate the work and boost its real-world influence.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LidarPainter: One-Step Away From Any Lidar View To Novel Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12114v1)
- **Authors**: Yuzhou Ji, Ke Ma, Hong Cai, Anchun Zhang, Lizhuang Ma, Xin Tan
- **Abstract**: Dynamic driving scene reconstruction is of great importance in fields like digital twin system and autonomous driving simulation. However, unacceptable degradation occurs when the view deviates from the input trajectory, leading to corrupted background and vehicle models. To improve reconstruction quality on novel trajectory, existing methods are subject to various limitations including inconsistency, deformation, and time consumption. This paper proposes LidarPainter, a one-step diffusion model that recovers consistent driving views from sparse LiDAR condition and artifact-corrupted renderings in real-time, enabling high-fidelity lane shifts in driving scene reconstruction. Extensive experiments show that LidarPainter outperforms state-of-the-art methods in speed, quality and resource efficiency, specifically 7 x faster than StreetCrafter with only one fifth of GPU memory required. LidarPainter also supports stylized generation using text prompts such as "foggy" and "night", allowing for a diverse expansion of the existing asset library.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LidarPainter, a novel one-step diffusion model for driving scene reconstruction. It aims to address limitations of existing methods that struggle with consistency, deformation, and computational cost when reconstructing scenes from sparse LiDAR data and artifact-corrupted renderings. LidarPainter uses a Latent Attention Fusion Module to effectively combine LiDAR's structural information with details from the corrupted renderings, enabling real-time, high-fidelity lane shifts in reconstructed driving scenes. The paper demonstrates that LidarPainter outperforms state-of-the-art methods in speed, quality, and resource efficiency.  It also supports stylized generation using text prompts, expanding the asset library's diversity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of a one-step diffusion model with a Latent Attention Fusion Module tailored for integrating sparse LiDAR data and artifact-ridden renderings.  While diffusion models and scene reconstruction from LiDAR data are established areas, the specific architecture and approach to fusion seem genuinely new. The paper directly addresses the limitations of prior work like StreetCrafter, which struggles with sparse LiDAR and detail preservation. The paper's one-step approach, directly mapping artifact and LiDAR to guidance images for 3D reconstruction is also somewhat novel, avoiding the intermediate video generation step which is often slow and resource-intensive.

*   **Significance:** The significance is moderate to high. The paper makes a practical improvement over current state-of-the-art methods (StreetCrafter). Specifically, the improvement in speed and reduction in GPU memory requirements (7x faster and 1/5th memory) make it more accessible for real-world applications, which is valuable. The ability to generate stylized scenes using text prompts further enhances the practical utility of the method.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is demonstrably faster and more memory efficient compared to the existing state-of-the-art.
    *   It generates higher-quality reconstructions with better detail and consistency.
    *   The incorporation of stylized generation offers a novel feature for asset library expansion.
    *   The paper includes thorough quantitative and qualitative results. The ablation studies clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of the Latent Attention Fusion Module.
    *   The paper is well-written and presents a clear explanation of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper, like many in the field, relies on a substantial amount of training data. The details on how the artifact-corrupted renderings are created are somewhat brief and could be elaborated upon, which can be useful for replication or understanding the limitations of this type of system.
    *   While speed and GPU memory have improved significantly, a real-time rendering solution may benefit from more investigation into the types of architectural designs that will offer the most benefit in a production environment.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7.5**. While the individual components (diffusion models, LiDAR rendering, scene reconstruction) are not groundbreaking, the combination and specific design choices (one-step approach, Latent Attention Fusion) represent a significant engineering contribution. The practical improvements in speed, memory efficiency, and reconstruction quality are demonstrably substantial. The ability to create stylistic variations with text prompts is a nice bonus.  The method addresses a clear and relevant problem in the autonomous driving and digital twin domains and is likely to be impactful in those fields, given it has demonstratable advantage over previous State-of-The-Art methods.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Block-based Symmetric Pruning and Fusion for Efficient Vision Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12125v1)
- **Authors**: Yi-Kuan Hsieh, Jun-Wei Hsieh, Xin Li, Yu-Ming Chang, Yu-Chee Tseng
- **Abstract**: Vision Transformer (ViT) has achieved impressive results across various vision tasks, yet its high computational cost limits practical applications. Recent methods have aimed to reduce ViT's $O(n^2)$ complexity by pruning unimportant tokens. However, these techniques often sacrifice accuracy by independently pruning query (Q) and key (K) tokens, leading to performance degradation due to overlooked token interactions. To address this limitation, we introduce a novel {\bf Block-based Symmetric Pruning and Fusion} for efficient ViT (BSPF-ViT) that optimizes the pruning of Q/K tokens jointly. Unlike previous methods that consider only a single direction, our approach evaluates each token and its neighbors to decide which tokens to retain by taking token interaction into account. The retained tokens are compressed through a similarity fusion step, preserving key information while reducing computational costs. The shared weights of Q/K tokens create a symmetric attention matrix, allowing pruning only the upper triangular part for speed up. BSPF-ViT consistently outperforms state-of-the-art ViT methods at all pruning levels, increasing ImageNet classification accuracy by 1.3% on DeiT-T and 2.0% on DeiT-S, while reducing computational overhead by 50%. It achieves 40% speedup with improved accuracy across various ViTs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces Block-based Symmetric Pruning and Fusion for efficient Vision Transformers (BSPF-ViT).  The method aims to reduce the computational complexity of ViTs by jointly optimizing the pruning of query (Q) and key (K) tokens. It employs a block-based pruning strategy, assessing the importance of each token and its neighbors before deciding which to retain, thus taking token interactions into account. The retained tokens are then compressed using a similarity fusion step. The shared weights between Q and K result in a symmetric attention matrix, enabling pruning of only the upper triangular part. The authors demonstrate that BSPF-ViT outperforms state-of-the-art ViT methods at various pruning levels, with improved ImageNet classification accuracy and reduced computational overhead.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the block-based symmetric pruning approach.  While token pruning and fusion are not entirely new concepts in ViT optimization, the paper proposes a specific combination of ideas that show promise. The key aspect of evaluating neighboring tokens during pruning, as opposed to making individual pruning decisions, is a sensible and potentially beneficial modification. The leveraging of shared weights to enforce symmetry and reduce computation is also a worthwhile optimization strategy.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper rests on its ability to reduce the computational cost of ViTs without significantly sacrificing accuracy. ViTs' quadratic complexity is a well-known bottleneck, and any method that can alleviate this issue is valuable. The reported performance gains (increased accuracy and reduced FLOPs) are substantial and contribute to the practicality of deploying ViTs in resource-constrained environments.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Sound Methodology:** The approach is well-motivated and clearly explained. The use of block-based pruning and similarity-based fusion is a logical way to preserve essential information during token reduction.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides convincing empirical evidence supporting the effectiveness of BSPF-ViT. The consistent outperformance of state-of-the-art methods across different ViT architectures (DeiT-T, DeiT-S, T2T-ViT, LV-ViT) is a major strength. Ablation studies justify the design choices (e.g., the importance of neighboring tokens).
    *   **Symmetry Exploitation:** The exploitation of symmetry for faster pruning, arising from shared Q/K weights, is a computationally appealing optimization.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:**  While the combination of block-based pruning, similarity fusion, and symmetry exploitation is novel, the individual components are not entirely new. A strong framing would position this as a contribution demonstrating how existing ideas could be successfully integrated.
    *   **Scope of Downstream Tasks:** The downstream task experiments focus on a relatively limited setup using SHViT as the backbone. More diverse applications of the optimized ViTs could strengthen the claims of generalizability.
    *   **Parameter Tuning:** The pruning ratio and other parameters require tuning. The method's sensitivity to hyperparameter settings is not exhaustively explored, though the ablation study provides some insight. This raises questions about how easily the method can be adapted to different tasks and datasets.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the results hold up and can be reproduced by other researchers, BSPF-ViT has the potential to become a valuable tool for deploying efficient ViTs. The improvements in accuracy and speed can make ViTs more accessible for applications where computational resources are limited. The framework can encourage further work on structured pruning and token fusion.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution by effectively combining different techniques to create an efficient and accurate ViT optimization method. The block-based approach, similarity fusion, and symmetry exploitation work together to preserve important information while reducing computational costs. The results demonstrate strong empirical performance, and the method has the potential to influence the design and deployment of ViTs in practical applications. However, a framing that acknowledges the pre-existing components and the requirement for some tuning temper the enthusiasm.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel *combination* of techniques that results in a noticeable performance improvement in a well-established problem. The strong experimental results and thoughtful ablation studies strengthen the claims. However, there is room for more extensive experimentation regarding sensitivity to hyperparameters and a more explicit acknowledgement of pre-existing token reduction strategies. While the individual components have connections to previous work, the method demonstrates good performance that warrants future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### HyDRA: A Hybrid Dual-Mode Network for Closed- and Open-Set RFFI with Optimized VMD
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12133v1)
- **Authors**: Hanwen Liu, Yuhe Huang, Yifeng Gong, Yanjie Zhai, Jiaxuan Lu
- **Abstract**: Device recognition is vital for security in wireless communication systems, particularly for applications like access control. Radio Frequency Fingerprint Identification (RFFI) offers a non-cryptographic solution by exploiting hardware-induced signal distortions. This paper proposes HyDRA, a Hybrid Dual-mode RF Architecture that integrates an optimized Variational Mode Decomposition (VMD) with a novel architecture based on the fusion of Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs), Transformers, and Mamba components, designed to support both closed-set and open-set classification tasks. The optimized VMD enhances preprocessing efficiency and classification accuracy by fixing center frequencies and using closed-form solutions. HyDRA employs the Transformer Dynamic Sequence Encoder (TDSE) for global dependency modeling and the Mamba Linear Flow Encoder (MLFE) for linear-complexity processing, adapting to varying conditions. Evaluation on public datasets demonstrates state-of-the-art (SOTA) accuracy in closed-set scenarios and robust performance in our proposed open-set classification method, effectively identifying unauthorized devices. Deployed on NVIDIA Jetson Xavier NX, HyDRA achieves millisecond-level inference speed with low power consumption, providing a practical solution for real-time wireless authentication in real-world environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HyDRA: A Hybrid Dual-Mode Network for Closed- and Open-Set RFFI with Optimized VMD":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes HyDRA, a novel radio frequency fingerprint identification (RFFI) framework. It addresses limitations in existing RFFI methods by integrating optimized Variational Mode Decomposition (VMD) and a reconfigurable dual-encoder architecture. The optimized VMD uses fixed center frequencies and closed-form solutions to reduce computational complexity and reconstruction losses compared to the traditional ADMM-based VMD. The dual-encoder dynamically switches between a Transformer Dynamic Sequence Encoder (TDSE) for high-precision tasks and a Mamba Linear Flow Encoder (MLFE) for efficient processing on resource-constrained devices. The framework supports both closed-set and open-set classification, with the latter employing a threshold-based discrimination mechanism to identify unauthorized devices. The results demonstrate state-of-the-art accuracy on public datasets and efficient deployment on NVIDIA Jetson Xavier NX.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits several novel aspects:
    *   **Optimized VMD:** The modification of VMD by fixing center frequencies and using closed-form solutions is a significant optimization, reducing computational load and improving accuracy by avoiding iterative solutions and reconstruction errors. This can be a key selling point.

    *   **Dual-Mode Encoder (TDSE/MLFE):** The hybrid architecture that combines Transformer-based and Mamba-based encoders allows the system to adapt to different requirements by choosing a trade-off between computational load and high accuracy. The integration of the Mamba architecture into RFFI appears to be a relatively unique element.

    *   **Open-Set RFFI:** While open-set recognition is a growing area, applying it specifically in the RFFI context with threshold discrimination adds a practical dimension for real-world security scenarios.
    *   **Edge Deployment:** Practical demonstration on a real-world edge device (Jetson Xavier NX) shows the applicability of their architecture.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses key challenges in RFFI, specifically enhancing accuracy, reducing computational complexity, and addressing the critical need for open-set recognition in practical wireless communication environments. The reported millisecond-level inference speed with low power consumption on the Jetson Xavier NX is a compelling result, demonstrating the practical applicability of the proposed approach for real-time wireless authentication.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-structured, clearly explains the proposed method, and provides sufficient technical details.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive, comparing HyDRA to several baseline methods and thoroughly evaluating the performance of different components of the framework.
    *   The paper addresses a practical problem with real-world implications.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the concept of dual-mode architecture is novel, the actual implementation and benefits derived (especially in different usage scenarios) could be further elaborated. For example, what's the power saving achieved when using Mamba instead of a Transformer? In what specific applications would using the low-power solution be preferred?
    *   The impact of the threshold value on the overall performance of Open-Set RFFI must be further emphasized and perhaps the method for automated selection can be proposed.
    *   Although the core contribution is the architecture, the VMD portion is still relying on knowing the symbol rate in advance, and if there is an offset on symbol rate or carrier frequency, its performance may deteriorate. Future study that covers practical consideration may worth more.

*   **Potential Influence:** The HyDRA framework has the potential to influence the RFFI field by providing a practical and efficient solution for device authentication in wireless communication systems. The optimized VMD and the dual-encoder architecture can inspire new research directions in RFFI. The open-set classification approach addresses a critical limitation of existing RFFI methods.
*   **Justification for Score:** I assign a score of 7.5 because the paper offers significant novel contributions to the field of RFFI, particularly through the optimized VMD, the dual-mode encoder, and the open-set classification approach. The real-world edge deployment is a significant strength. However, the paper could benefit from more detailed analysis and comparative results concerning the conditions, power saving, and trade-offs of the dual-encoder. Additionally, the reliance on symbol rate information in the VMD portion as the limitation of the study somewhat weaken its significance. This makes it a good, solid advance but not a transformative leap.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### RiemannLoRA: A Unified Riemannian Framework for Ambiguity-Free LoRA Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12142v1)
- **Authors**: Vladimir Bogachev, Vladimir Aletov, Alexander Molozhavenko, Denis Bobkov, Vera Soboleva, Aibek Alanov, Maxim Rakhuba
- **Abstract**: Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) has become a widely adopted standard for parameter-efficient fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs), significantly reducing memory and computational demands. However, challenges remain, including finding optimal initialization strategies or mitigating overparametrization in low-rank matrix factorization. In this work, we propose a novel approach that addresses both of the challenges simultaneously within a unified framework. Our method treats a set of fixed-rank LoRA matrices as a smooth manifold. Considering adapters as elements on this manifold removes overparametrization, while determining the direction of the fastest loss decrease along the manifold provides initialization. Special care is taken to obtain numerically stable and computationally efficient implementation of our method, using best practices from numerical linear algebra and Riemannian optimization. Experimental results on LLM and diffusion model architectures demonstrate that RiemannLoRA consistently improves both convergence speed and final performance over standard LoRA and its state-of-the-art modifications.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RiemannLoRA, a novel approach to parameter-efficient fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs) based on Riemannian optimization. It addresses two key challenges in LoRA: optimal initialization and mitigating overparametrization. The method treats LoRA matrices as elements on a smooth manifold, eliminating overparametrization and enabling geometrically meaningful initialization. They use numerically stable and efficient implementations, leveraging best practices from numerical linear algebra and Riemannian optimization. Experimental results on LLMs and diffusion models demonstrate improved convergence speed and final performance compared to standard LoRA and its modifications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using Riemannian optimization in LoRA is novel and addresses a significant problem in parameter-efficient tuning. Treating LoRA matrices as elements on a fixed-rank manifold and optimizing directly on that manifold offers a way to avoid overparametrization and the associated ambiguities. The initialisation scheme derived from tangent space considerations is also a good contribution.

*   **Significance:** LoRA has become a standard tool for fine-tuning LLMs. Improving its performance and efficiency can have a broad impact. The paper's findings of faster convergence and better performance are significant, especially given the growing need for efficient fine-tuning techniques. The use of diffusion models to demonstrate the usefulness is also good.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The Riemannian framework provides a mathematically sound approach to address LoRA's limitations.
    *   The locally optimal initialization (LOI) method is well-motivated and shown to be effective.
    *   The paper provides a detailed algorithm and discusses computational efficiency.
    *   Experiments are conducted on relevant LLMs and a diffusion model benchmark to support the claims.
    *   The paper also handles nuances involving how the orthogonal factorisation is done on the LoRA matrices.
    *   There is careful hyper-parameter tuning for different approaches and the ablation is done reasonably well.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper is somewhat dense and requires a good understanding of Riemannian geometry and optimization.  Making the core ideas more accessible would improve its impact.
    *   While the experiments show improvements, the gains are not always dramatic. A more extensive empirical evaluation across a wider range of tasks and models would strengthen the claims.
    *   The paper could delve deeper into the theoretical properties of RiemannLoRA, such as convergence guarantees.
    *   The Adam optimizer is very similar to the conventional version. Stronger evaluation of alternatives may further bolster the results.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and robust fine-tuning techniques for LLMs. The idea of leveraging Riemannian geometry could inspire new approaches in this area. However, wider adoption will depend on the ease of implementation and the magnitude of performance gains observed in diverse settings.  The experimental improvements also aren't consistently that huge and need to be further improved.

*Theorems and Proofs*:
These elements are present and add a degree of formal rigor.

*Clarity of Writing*: The paper's technical nature can be challenging. There's a need for more intuitive explanations to broaden accessibility.

**Overall Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to improve LoRA using Riemannian optimization and provides a meaningful direction in parameter efficient tuning. The results demonstrate improved convergence and performance in some settings.  The primary reason the score isn't higher is the limited magnitude of experimental gains, the technical complexity, and the need for more extensive evaluation. The paper needs wider accessibility to the theory, especially given the limited improvements.

Score: 7.5

### SmokeSVD: Smoke Reconstruction from A Single View via Progressive Novel View Synthesis and Refinement with Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12156v1)
- **Authors**: Chen Li, Shanshan Dong, Sheng Qiu, Jianmin Han, Zan Gao, Kemeng Huang, Taku Komura
- **Abstract**: Reconstructing dynamic fluids from sparse views is a long-standing and challenging problem, due to the severe lack of 3D information from insufficient view coverage. While several pioneering approaches have attempted to address this issue using differentiable rendering or novel view synthesis, they are often limited by time-consuming optimization and refinement processes under ill-posed conditions. To tackle above challenges, we propose SmokeSVD, an efficient and effective framework to progressively generate and reconstruct dynamic smoke from a single video by integrating both the powerful generative capabilities from diffusion models and physically guided consistency optimization towards realistic appearance and dynamic evolution. Specifically, we first propose a physically guided side-view synthesizer based on diffusion models, which explicitly incorporates divergence and gradient guidance of velocity fields to generate visually realistic and spatio-temporally consistent side-view images frame by frame, significantly alleviating the ill-posedness of single-view reconstruction without imposing additional constraints. Subsequently, we determine a rough estimation of density field from the pair of front-view input and side-view synthetic image, and further refine 2D blurry novel-view images and 3D coarse-grained density field through an iterative process that progressively renders and enhances the images from increasing novel viewing angles, generating high-quality multi-view image sequences. Finally, we reconstruct and estimate the fine-grained density field, velocity field, and smoke source via differentiable advection by leveraging the Navier-Stokes equations. Extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments show that our approach achieves high-quality reconstruction and outperforms previous state-of-the-art techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SmokeSVD, a novel framework for reconstructing dynamic smoke from a single-view video. The approach leverages a multi-stage process integrating diffusion models and physics-based constraints.  First, a physically guided diffusion model synthesizes a side-view video sequence from the front-view input.  Then, a coarse 3D density field is generated, and the method iteratively refines novel view images from different angles using a novel view refinement module. Finally, the density field, velocity field, and smoke source are reconstructed using differentiable advection, guided by Navier-Stokes equations. The framework enables smoke re-simulation and artist-driven control.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its multi-stage approach to single-view smoke reconstruction, combining diffusion models, novel view synthesis, and physics-based constraints.  While individual components (diffusion models, differentiable rendering) are not entirely new, their integration within a progressive framework with specific guidance modules for smoke reconstruction constitutes a significant contribution. The side-view synthesizer guided by velocity and spatial information and the iterative novel view refinement module are potentially novel and effective contributions. The novelty hinges on the particular architecture and loss functions used within the different modules, and the way they are combined.

* **Significance:** The significance of SmokeSVD resides in its ability to generate high-quality smoke reconstructions from sparse, single-view data, a long-standing challenge in computer graphics and vision. Successfully addressing this problem unlocks various applications, including re-simulation, visual effects, and potentially atmospheric modeling. Compared to previous approaches, the claim of balancing reconstruction quality and computational efficiency makes it potentially more practical. The claim of enabling novel view synthesis robustly enough for applications such as 3D Gaussian Splatting is also significant.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The integration of diffusion models with physics-based constraints seems promising, leading to visually realistic and temporally coherent results.
    *   The multi-stage approach allows leveraging the strengths of both generative models and optimization-based techniques.
    *   The ablations studies, detailed in the supplementary material, are crucial for validating the contribution of each component in the framework.
    *   The ability to enable re-simulation and artist-driven control increases the practical value of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a clean background and consistent lighting may limit its applicability in complex real-world scenarios. While they mention it as a limitation, such a restriction could be a very significant one.
    *   The potential for error propagation in the multi-stage process needs to be carefully addressed. Any inaccuracies during the side-view synthesis could cascade into subsequent steps.
    *   The evaluation is somewhat limited to synthetic and controlled real-world datasets, so its generalizability to more challenging scenarios remains to be seen. Details of the dataset are lacking.
    *   It's difficult to evaluate the significance of the approach without a deep dive into the architectural details and the loss functions being used.

* **Potential Influence:** If the approach robustly delivers on its promises of high-quality reconstructions and efficient computation, it could become a valuable tool for visual effects artists and researchers working on fluid simulation and understanding. The use of the generated novel views for downstream tasks such as 3D Gaussian Splatting could influence research in neural rendering, too.

**Justification:**

While SmokeSVD presents a novel approach to a challenging problem, a few key areas warrant a slightly lower rating. First, the framework leverages existing components. A higher score would require a groundbreaking method or a significantly superior performance to existing state-of-the-art solutions. Second, the current version of the model requires relatively strict conditions of consistent lighting and clean backgrounds, which seriously restricts its applicability to the real world. Further research is required to enhance its real-world application. However, the clear potential of the progressive synthesis strategy and the thorough experimental analysis (particularly in the supplementary material) make this paper a valuable contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fine-Grained Image Recognition from Scratch with Teacher-Guided Data Augmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12157v1)
- **Authors**: Edwin Arkel Rios, Fernando Mikael, Oswin Gosal, Femiloye Oyerinde, Hao-Chun Liang, Bo-Cheng Lai, Min-Chun Hu
- **Abstract**: Fine-grained image recognition (FGIR) aims to distinguish visually similar sub-categories within a broader class, such as identifying bird species. While most existing FGIR methods rely on backbones pretrained on large-scale datasets like ImageNet, this dependence limits adaptability to resource-constrained environments and hinders the development of task-specific architectures tailored to the unique challenges of FGIR. In this work, we challenge the conventional reliance on pretrained models by demonstrating that high-performance FGIR systems can be trained entirely from scratch. We introduce a novel training framework, TGDA, that integrates data-aware augmentation with weak supervision via a fine-grained-aware teacher model, implemented through knowledge distillation. This framework unlocks the design of task-specific and hardware-aware architectures, including LRNets for low-resolution FGIR and ViTFS, a family of Vision Transformers optimized for efficient inference. Extensive experiments across three FGIR benchmarks over diverse settings involving low-resolution and high-resolution inputs show that our method consistently matches or surpasses state-of-the-art pretrained counterparts. In particular, in the low-resolution setting, LRNets trained with TGDA improve accuracy by up to 23\% over prior methods while requiring up to 20.6x less parameters, lower FLOPs, and significantly less training data. Similarly, ViTFS-T can match the performance of a ViT B-16 pretrained on ImageNet-21k while using 15.3x fewer trainable parameters and requiring orders of magnitudes less data. These results highlight TGDA's potential as an adaptable alternative to pretraining, paving the way for more efficient fine-grained vision systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of fine-grained image recognition (FGIR), focusing on training models from scratch, without relying on pre-trained backbones (typically ImageNet). The authors introduce a novel two-stage training framework called Teacher-Guided Data Augmentation (TGDA). TGDA uses a fine-grained-aware teacher model to generate part attention maps (PAMs), which drive diverse data augmentations. These augmentations then supervise the training of a student model from random initialization. The paper explores two specific architectures optimized for TGDA: LRNets, designed for low-resolution FGIR, and ViTFS, a Batch Normalization-based Vision Transformer optimized for hardware efficiency. Experiments demonstrate that TGDA-trained models can match or surpass the performance of pretrained state-of-the-art methods while requiring significantly less computational resources and training data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper has several aspects of novelty:

*   **Training from Scratch with TGDA:** The core idea of training FGIR models effectively from scratch is significant. While knowledge distillation and data augmentation are not new individually, their integration via the proposed TGDA framework, particularly with the PAM-driven augmentation, presents a novel approach.
*   **LRNets for Low-Resolution FGIR:** The architecture of LRNets is purpose-built for low-resolution images, a scenario often overlooked in standard benchmarks.  The specific modifications to ResNet to preserve fine-grained information at lower resolutions constitute a novel architectural contribution.
*   **ViTFS for Hardware Efficiency:** While Batch Normalization is widely used, the explicit design of ViTFS with BatchNorm for improved deployment on specific hardware (where LayerNorm is inefficient) addresses a practical limitation. The architecture modifications to make ViTs more amenable to training with limited data and compatible with different hardware is a valuable engineering contribution.

**Significance:** The paper's significance lies in several areas:

*   **Resource Efficiency:** The reduced reliance on pretraining and the optimization for both low-resolution scenarios and hardware constraints make FGIR more accessible, particularly to researchers and practitioners with limited resources. The reduction in parameters, FLOPs, and data requirements addresses a crucial bottleneck in deep learning research.
*   **Architectural Flexibility:** By removing the dependence on pre-trained backbones, the method enables the design and exploration of task-specific architectures tailored to the unique challenges of FGIR, rather than adapting existing architectures pretrained for general image recognition. This allows for potentially more efficient and effective models.
*   **Practical Impact:** Low-resolution FGIR has several real-world applications. Creating models tailored to this setting can significantly impact fields like wildlife monitoring and similar tasks where high resolution imagery is not readily available.  Similarly, hardware-aware design directly addresses deployment limitations in resource-constrained environments.
*   **Challenging Pretraining Paradigm:** The paper's successful training of high-performance models from scratch calls into question the ubiquitous reliance on pretraining. This could lead to a re-evaluation of transfer learning paradigms.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of current FGIR methods and the need for training from scratch.
*   **Well-Defined Method:** TGDA is described in detail, and the design choices are well-motivated.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering multiple datasets and settings (high-resolution and low-resolution). The comparative analysis against state-of-the-art methods is thorough and convincing. The ablation studies isolating the impact of the different components of TGDA adds additional value.
*   **Hardware Considerations:** Addressing the hardware limitations of standard vision transformers and designing ViTFS with BatchNorm for better real-world deployability is a great idea.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:** The two-stage training process with the teacher-student framework adds complexity, which could make it more difficult to reproduce compared to single-stage training.
*   **Teacher Model Dependence:** The performance of the student model is inherently tied to the performance of the teacher model. The selection and training of the teacher model is an important consideration.  While the paper specifies the teachers used, more detail on the sensitivity to the teacher’s architecture or hyperparameters would be valuable.
*   **Limited Architectural Exploration:** While LRNets are specifically designed for low-resolution FGIR, the architectural modifications are relatively modest extensions of existing ResNet architectures. Further exploration of more radically different architectures may be beneficial.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The distillation temperature and weight for the distillation loss can be sensitive. More exploration is needed for a more robust framework.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to FGIR by demonstrating that high-performance models can be trained effectively from scratch. The TGDA framework is novel and well-executed.  The architectures designed for low-resolution and hardware-constrained scenarios show the practical benefits of the method.  The comprehensive experimental results are compelling and provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of TGDA. While the method has some complexity and reliance on a well-trained teacher model, the resource efficiency and architectural flexibility it enables are highly valuable.

### RODS: Robust Optimization Inspired Diffusion Sampling for Detecting and Reducing Hallucination in Generative Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12201v1)
- **Authors**: Yiqi Tian, Pengfei Jin, Mingze Yuan, Na Li, Bo Zeng, Quanzheng Li
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have achieved state-of-the-art performance in generative modeling, yet their sampling procedures remain vulnerable to hallucinations, often stemming from inaccuracies in score approximation. In this work, we reinterpret diffusion sampling through the lens of optimization and introduce RODS (Robust Optimization-inspired Diffusion Sampler), a novel method that detects and corrects high-risk sampling steps using geometric cues from the loss landscape. RODS enforces smoother sampling trajectories and adaptively adjusts perturbations, reducing hallucinations without retraining and at minimal additional inference cost. Experiments on AFHQv2, FFHQ, and 11k-hands demonstrate that RODS improves both sampling fidelity and robustness, detecting over 70% of hallucinated samples and correcting more than 25%, all while avoiding the introduction of new artifacts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RODS (Robust Optimization-inspired Diffusion Sampler), a novel method to detect and mitigate hallucinations in diffusion models without retraining. RODS reinterprets diffusion sampling as an optimization process and leverages robust optimization principles. It explores the local neighborhood of each sampling step to detect regions with high curvature or instability, which are indicative of potential hallucinations. When such regions are detected, RODS dynamically adjusts the sampling direction using either Sharpness-Aware Sampling (SAS) or Curvature-Aware Sampling (CAS) to ensure robustness. Experiments on AFHQv2, FFHQ, and 11k-hands datasets demonstrate that RODS can detect a significant percentage of hallucinated samples and correct them, with minimal additional inference cost and without introducing new artifacts. The key contribution lies in framing diffusion sampling as a continuation method and applying robust optimization principles to address the inherent uncertainty in score function approximation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in two areas: (1) The reinterpretation of diffusion sampling as a continuation method in numerical optimization. While connections between diffusion and optimization have been explored before, this specific framing provides a new lens for understanding sampling failures and applying optimization techniques. (2) The integration of robust optimization principles, specifically SAS and CAS, into the diffusion sampling process. This approach directly addresses the problem of inaccurate score function approximations, which are a primary source of hallucinations.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a critical issue in generative modeling: hallucinations. While hallucination is a well-recognized challenge, this paper offers a practical and effective solution that doesn't require retraining the model, making it readily applicable to existing pre-trained diffusion models. The performance gains are significant, especially in terms of reducing hallucination rates and introducing fewer new artifacts, making the generated content more reliable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Principled Approach:** Grounding the method in optimization theory provides a solid theoretical foundation.
    *   **Practicality:** RODS is a plug-and-play method that doesn't require retraining, increasing its accessibility and real-world applicability.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Extensive experiments on diverse datasets (AFHQv2, FFHQ, and 11k-hands) demonstrate the effectiveness and generalizability of the approach.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies thoroughly analyze the impact of different components of RODS, providing valuable insights into its behavior.
    *   **Clear Explanation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the motivation, method, and results.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Manual Hallucination Annotation:** Although the authors employed lab members for human annotation of generated examples to ascertain the existence of hallucination, it may be biased and time-consuming.
    *   **Dependence on Threshold Tuning:** The performance of RODS relies on tuning the detection threshold (€), which could be sensitive to the specific dataset and application. While the paper provides guidelines, a more adaptive or automatic threshold selection mechanism would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Scope:**  The empirical validation is limited to unconditional image generation. While the authors claim that generalizability to text-to-image or conditional generation tasks should be straightforward, this remains to be demonstrated. The efficacy of RODS in more complex settings, such as controlling the semantics and/or the overall composition of generated images, is unknown.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper states minimal computational overhead, probing the local neighbourhood can be expensive, especially if M is high dimensional. A more careful and comparative analysis needs to be made to justify that it is computationally efficient for high dimensional images.
    *   **Lack of Comparison with State-of-the-Art:** It is unclear how RODS compares with other state-of-the-art hallucination mitigation techniques. A formal comparison with existing techniques is necessary to better quantify the efficacy and usefulness of the proposed method.
    *   **Limited Novelty of SAS and CAS:** The core idea of Robust Optimization itself is well-established. A greater clarity and explanation for using SAS and CAS, and when to use each, needs to be provided.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of generative modeling by providing a practical and effective solution to the hallucination problem. It could inspire further research into the application of optimization principles to improve the robustness and reliability of diffusion models. The framework can be extended to other generative models to test its generalisability.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses of the paper, a score of 7 is appropriate. The idea of framing diffusion as a continuation process is interesting and leads to the RODS method. The empirical results and clear ablation studies lend credence to the method's efficacy. However, the limitations in scope, dependence on threshold tuning, lack of comparison to state-of-the-art methods, dependence on human annotation, and limited novelty of SAS and CAS prevent a higher score.

### Toward Efficient SpMV in Sparse LLMs via Block Extraction and Compressed Storage
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12205v1)
- **Authors**: Junqing Lin, Jingwei Sun, Mingge Lu, Guangzhong Sun
- **Abstract**: Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication (SpMV) has become a critical performance bottleneck in the local deployment of sparse Large Language Models (LLMs), where inference predominantly operates on workloads during the decoder phase with a batch size of one. Existing SpMV kernels and sparse matrix formats, originally designed for scientific computing, fail to exploit the unique structure patterns inherent in sparse LLMs, resulting in suboptimal performance and excessive storage overhead. This paper presents EC-SpMV, a GPU-optimized SpMV approach for accelerating sparse LLM inference. EC-SpMV introduces (1) a hierarchical block extraction algorithm that captures multiple granularities of block structures within sparse LLMs, and (2) a novel compressed sparse format (EC-CSR) that employs delta indexing to reduce storage overhead and enhance memory access efficiency. Evaluated on real sparse weight matrices from LLaMA and OPT models, EC-SpMV achieves up to 6.44x speedup over state-of-the-art SpMV libraries and reduces storage overhead by up to 55.4% compared to CSR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EC-SpMV, a GPU-optimized approach to accelerate Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication (SpMV) specifically tailored for sparse Large Language Models (LLMs) deployed on consumer-grade GPUs.  EC-SpMV addresses the challenges of suboptimal performance and storage overhead associated with existing SpMV kernels and sparse matrix formats. It achieves this through two main innovations: (1) a hierarchical block extraction algorithm that captures multi-granularity block structures within sparse LLMs, and (2) a novel compressed sparse format (EC-CSR) employing delta indexing to reduce storage overhead and improve memory access efficiency.  Experimental results on real sparse weight matrices from LLaMA and OPT models demonstrate significant speedups over state-of-the-art SpMV libraries and reduced storage overhead compared to standard CSR.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its integrated approach designed specifically for the unique characteristics of sparse LLMs. The combination of hierarchical block extraction and the EC-CSR format is a contribution to the field. While tiling and compression are known techniques in isolation, the specific application and optimization for LLM inference, especially on consumer GPUs, is reasonably novel. The hierarchical block extraction tailored for LLM sparsity patterns seems a significant advancement over existing techniques. However, it's essential to ascertain if similar techniques exist, perhaps in a less formal context, for optimizing performance in related domains.
* **Significance:** The significance of the work is in improving the feasibility of deploying large language models locally on consumer-grade GPUs. Achieving both speedup and storage reduction is a crucial step toward making LLMs more accessible and usable for individual users, addressing data privacy concerns and enabling user-specific customizations. If the approach provides significant performance advantages, it could be widely adopted.  However, the claims hinge on experimental results and a thorough comparison with the current state-of-the-art.
* **Strengths:**
    * The paper addresses a relevant and increasingly important problem: efficient inference of LLMs on resource-constrained devices.
    * The proposed EC-SpMV approach shows promising results, demonstrating significant speedups and storage reductions compared to existing methods.
    * The paper clearly outlines the proposed techniques and provides sufficient experimental details.
    * The two-pronged approach of block extraction coupled with a specific sparse storage format seems like a good design.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The approach seems heavily optimized for the sparsity patterns induced by SparseGPT. The broader applicability to other pruning methods (e.g., magnitude-based pruning) or different types of sparsity might be limited or require modification.
    * The runtime overhead of the preprocessing steps (block extraction and format conversion) needs more in-depth analysis. Although stated that these steps are done offline once, a too-large time overhead would make the whole method less competitive.
    * The evaluation, while comprehensive, should include a more diverse range of LLMs and pruning methods. Also, it is unclear to me if the performance boost comes from just the block extraction, or the compression, or load balancing strategies.
    * While the paper describes memory coalescing and vectorized memory access optimizations, the details are somewhat brief. A more detailed explanation of how the EC-CSR format facilitates these optimizations on GPUs would be beneficial.
    * The greedy approach of hierarchical block extraction lacks optimality guarantees. While the experimental results are promising, a theoretical analysis would strengthen the paper.
* **Potential Influence:**  If the performance and storage benefits can be consistently demonstrated across various models and pruning techniques, EC-SpMV could become a standard optimization technique for sparse LLM inference.  The paper could inspire further research on sparse matrix formats and algorithms tailored for the unique characteristics of neural networks.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of efficient LLM inference.  The combination of hierarchical block extraction and the EC-CSR format is a well-motivated and effective approach. The experimental results demonstrate clear improvements over existing methods. However, there are some limitations regarding the generalizability of the approach and the depth of analysis. The current score is a 7, but future refinements and expansions could potentially increase this rating.

### BuildEvo: Designing Building Energy Consumption Forecasting Heuristics via LLM-driven Evolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12207v1)
- **Authors**: Subin Lin, Chuanbo Hua
- **Abstract**: Accurate building energy forecasting is essential, yet traditional heuristics often lack precision, while advanced models can be opaque and struggle with generalization by neglecting physical principles. This paper introduces BuildEvo, a novel framework that uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically design effective and interpretable energy prediction heuristics. Within an evolutionary process, BuildEvo guides LLMs to construct and enhance heuristics by systematically incorporating physical insights from building characteristics and operational data (e.g., from the Building Data Genome Project 2). Evaluations show BuildEvo achieves state-of-the-art performance on benchmarks, offering improved generalization and transparent prediction logic. This work advances the automated design of robust, physically grounded heuristics, promoting trustworthy models for complex energy systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BUILDEVO, a novel framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) within an evolutionary algorithm (EA) to automatically design accurate and interpretable building energy forecasting heuristics. BUILDEVO aims to bridge the gap between complex, "black-box" deep learning models and simpler, yet less accurate, traditional heuristics.  The framework iteratively generates, evaluates, and refines heuristics using observational data, guided by underlying physical principles of building energy consumption.  The core components include LLM-driven heuristic generation, a physical insights feedback loop (PIFL) to incorporate building characteristics and operational data, and cross-generation elite sampling (CGES) to enhance exploration.  The authors evaluate BUILDEVO on the Building Data Genome Project 2 (BDGP2) dataset and demonstrate competitive forecasting accuracy and improved generalization compared to established baselines. A key selling point is the interpretability of the generated Python code-based heuristics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using LLMs for automated heuristic design is not entirely new, as the paper acknowledges related work in automated algorithm design. However, the *application* to building energy forecasting, with a specific emphasis on incorporating physical knowledge and generating *interpretable* heuristics, distinguishes it. The PIFL and CGES components are also contributions, though evolutionary algorithms have used techniques to maintain diversity of the populations before. The novelty is moderate, lying primarily in the problem domain and the integrated approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem: balancing accuracy, interpretability, and generalization in building energy forecasting. Current methods often lack one or more of these desirable properties.  BUILDEVO's ability to generate transparent and physically-grounded heuristics has the potential to improve trust and adoption of energy forecasting models, which could have real-world impacts on energy efficiency and sustainability. Furthermore, the work suggests a broader paradigm for generating domain-specific rules for complex systems via LLM-guided evolution, which is significant.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined methodology.
    *   The use of LLMs within an EA framework is a sound approach to combining generative capabilities with structured search.
    *   The inclusion of a Physical Insights Feedback Loop (PIFL) and Cross-Generation Elite Sampling (CGES) is well-motivated to address limitations in traditional EA frameworks.
    *   Empirical results on a relevant benchmark dataset demonstrate competitive performance.
    *   Emphasizes the interpretability of the generated heuristics.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments, while showing competitive results, don't clearly demonstrate *superiority* over all baselines across all building types. Deeper analysis of *why* BUILDEVO excels in certain situations would be valuable.
    *   The interpretability claim, while stated, would be strengthened by providing a concrete example of a generated heuristic and discussing its physical meaning.
    *   The paper mentions the use of Google's Gemini model. Further details about the prompt engineering process and any challenges faced during LLM interaction are missing. The reliance on one specific, potentially evolving, LLM might limit reproducibility or adaptability of the method.
    *   The paper would benefit from further ablations. What is the relative importance of the PIFL and CGES. How well does each contribute to the overall approach?
    *   The performance of PIFL might have significant variations across different types of buildings with different amounts of data available.
    *   The evolutionary algorithm has a cost. How much computation is involved?

*   **Potential Influence:** If proven robust and generalizable, BUILDEVO could influence how building energy forecasting models are designed and deployed, particularly in situations where transparency and physical grounding are crucial. It could also inspire similar approaches in other domains where combining data-driven methods with domain knowledge is critical.

*   **Justification for Score:** BUILDEVO makes a meaningful contribution by addressing a critical problem with a novel approach and demonstrating promising empirical results. It combines LLMs and Evolutionary Algorithms, generating interpretable heuristics, that are particularly well grounded in physical principles for energy consumption.
    However, the paper is not without flaws. The experimental section could be strengthened with additional ablations and more in-depth analysis of the generated heuristics. The reliance on a single LLM and the lack of detailed information about the prompt engineering process also limit the paper's replicability and generalizability.

### Xiangqi-R1: Enhancing Spatial Strategic Reasoning in LLMs for Chinese Chess via Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12215v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhao Chen, Shuochen Liu, Yuanjie Lyu, Chao Zhang, Jiayao Shi, Tong Xu
- **Abstract**: Game playing has long served as a fundamental benchmark for evaluating Artificial General Intelligence (AGI). While Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities in general reasoning, their effectiveness in spatial strategic reasoning, which is critical for complex and fully observable board games, remains insufficiently explored. In this work, we adopt Chinese Chess (Xiangqi) as a challenging and rich testbed due to its intricate rules and spatial complexity. To advance LLMs' strategic competence in such environments, we propose a training framework tailored to Xiangqi, built upon a large-scale dataset of five million board-move pairs enhanced with expert annotations and engine evaluations. Building on this foundation, we introduce Xiangqi-R1, a 7B-parameter model trained in multi-stage manner: (1) fine-tuning for legal move prediction to capture basic spatial rules, (2) incorporating strategic annotations to improve decision-making, and (3) applying reinforcement learning via Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with multi-dimensional reward signals to enhance reasoning stability. Our Experimental results indicate that, despite their size and power, general-purpose LLMs struggle to achieve satisfactory performance in these tasks. Compared to general-purpose LLMs, Xiangqi-R1 greatly advances with an 18% rise in move legality and a 22% boost in analysis accuracy. Our results point to a promising path for creating general strategic intelligence in spatially complex areas.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Xiangqi-R1, a 7B-parameter language model (LLM) specifically trained for Chinese Chess (Xiangqi).  The authors argue that Xiangqi poses a greater challenge for LLMs in spatial strategic reasoning compared to International Chess due to its unique rules and spatial constraints. The paper proposes a three-stage training framework: 1) supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on a large dataset of board-move pairs to learn basic spatial rules, 2) incorporating strategic annotations (human commentaries and engine evaluations) to improve decision-making, and 3) reinforcement learning (RL) via Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with multi-dimensional reward signals to enhance reasoning stability. The results show that Xiangqi-R1 significantly outperforms general-purpose LLMs, including much larger models, in both move legality and analysis accuracy. The authors conclude that their approach shows a promising path toward creating general strategic intelligence in spatially complex areas.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has a moderate degree of novelty.  While the individual components (SFT, RL, using game engines for annotations) have been used before in training LLMs for games, the combination of these elements, tailored explicitly for Xiangqi and using a carefully curated dataset and the GRPO reinforcement learning framework, constitutes a novel contribution.  The focus on Xiangqi itself as a challenging testbed is also a valuable aspect, as most previous LLM-based game playing research focused on International Chess.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in several aspects. First, it tackles a relevant problem: the limitations of general-purpose LLMs in spatial strategic reasoning. Second, it demonstrates that domain-specific training and careful reward design can significantly boost performance in a complex, rule-based environment.  Third, it introduces a new evaluation framework and dataset for Xiangqi.  Fourth, the ablation studies clearly demonstrate the value of each component (move reward, analysis reward) in the overall framework, which strengthens the contributions.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined problem:**  The paper clearly identifies a weakness of current LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive approach:**  The three-stage training framework is well-motivated and addresses different aspects of spatial strategic reasoning.
    *   **High-quality dataset:** The careful curation and annotation of the Xiangqi dataset, along with its public release, is a significant asset.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** The paper presents clear and convincing evidence that Xiangqi-R1 outperforms general-purpose LLMs, even those with substantially more parameters. The detailed ablation studies are particularly valuable.
    *   **Clear writing and organization:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.
    *   **Detailed Appendices:** Providing implementation details and hyperparameter settings enhances reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Model size:** While achieving impressive results with a 7B model is commendable, the question remains how well this approach scales to larger models. The leap from a 7B parameter model to larger models might present unforeseen challenges.
    *   **Limited evaluation metrics:** While the paper uses several relevant metrics, a deeper analysis of strategic decision-making, beyond legality and analysis accuracy, would further strengthen the evaluation.  For example, evaluating the diversity of moves or how well the model adapts to different playing styles would be beneficial.
    *   **Dependency on Pikafish:** The reliance on Pikafish for generating annotations and evaluation scores introduces a potential bias.  Although Pikafish is a strong engine, it's not perfect, and its evaluations could influence the model's learning. While the human expert data mitigate this, directly using human analysis would be valuable.
    *   **Limited Generalization Scope:** While demonstrating results on Xiangqi is impactful, the lack of generalization to other related tasks limits the overall significance. It is unclear how easily the approach could be transferred to related board games.
    *   **Analysis depth could be expanded:** The depth of the analysis performed by the model itself, and how this is evaluated, could be expanded. It is hard to determine whether the model truly "understands" the board positions or merely regurgitates memorized sequences, though the increased accuracy implies it has learned beyond that.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence research in the following areas:
    *   Training LLMs for strategic reasoning in complex environments.
    *   Using game engines and expert annotations to improve LLM performance.
    *   Developing specialized LLMs for board games beyond International Chess.
    *   Creating better evaluation frameworks for LLMs in spatial reasoning tasks.

*   **Justification for score:** This paper provides a solid contribution to the field, bridging the gap between LLMs and complex strategic reasoning tasks in a challenging board game. The novel combination of techniques, the carefully curated dataset, and the strong experimental results warrant a relatively high score. While the limitations, especially around scaling and evaluation scope, prevent a higher score, the paper's potential to influence future research is undeniable.
