### MGFFD-VLM: Multi-Granularity Prompt Learning for Face Forgery Detection with VLM
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12232v1)
- **Authors**: Tao Chen, Jingyi Zhang, Decheng Liu, Chunlei Peng
- **Abstract**: Recent studies have utilized visual large language models (VLMs) to answer not only "Is this face a forgery?" but also "Why is the face a forgery?" These studies introduced forgery-related attributes, such as forgery location and type, to construct deepfake VQA datasets and train VLMs, achieving high accuracy while providing human-understandable explanatory text descriptions. However, these methods still have limitations. For example, they do not fully leverage face quality-related attributes, which are often abnormal in forged faces, and they lack effective training strategies for forgery-aware VLMs. In this paper, we extend the VQA dataset to create DD-VQA+, which features a richer set of attributes and a more diverse range of samples. Furthermore, we introduce a novel forgery detection framework, MGFFD-VLM, which integrates an Attribute-Driven Hybrid LoRA Strategy to enhance the capabilities of Visual Large Language Models (VLMs). Additionally, our framework incorporates Multi-Granularity Prompt Learning and a Forgery-Aware Training Strategy. By transforming classification and forgery segmentation results into prompts, our method not only improves forgery classification but also enhances interpretability. To further boost detection performance, we design multiple forgery-related auxiliary losses. Experimental results demonstrate that our approach surpasses existing methods in both text-based forgery judgment and analysis, achieving superior accuracy.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MGFFD-VLM: Multi-Granularity Prompt Learning for Face Forgery Detection with VLM".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework called MGFFD-VLM for face forgery detection using Visual Large Language Models (VLMs). It tackles the limitations of existing VLM-based methods, which primarily rely on manually annotated deepfake VQA datasets with limited diversity and struggle to effectively focus VLMs on forgery-specific features. The authors address these limitations by:

1.  **Extending the VQA dataset (DD-VQA+)**: They create a richer dataset (DD-VQA+) that includes additional face quality attributes (clarity, visibility, etc.) that are often abnormal in forged faces. It is extended to incorporate forgery locations and attribute in a self-supervised manner.
2.  **Attribute-Driven Hybrid LoRA Strategy**: They propose a novel training strategy (Attribute-Driven Hybrid LoRA Strategy) that leverages image quality attributes to dynamically select LoRA experts, guiding the VLM to focus on forgery-specific features.
3.  **Multi-Granularity Prompt Learning**: They introduce a multi-granularity prompt learning technique that transforms classification and forgery segmentation results into prompts, enhancing both the detection accuracy and the interpretability of the model. The framework utilizes classification/segmentation and prompts for better forgery-related cue extraction.
4.  **Forgery-Aware Training Strategy**: Finally, the framework incorporates Forgery-Aware Training Strategy, increase the perception ability of forgery cues through a progressive learning strategy and carefully designed loss functions.

The authors demonstrate through experiments that MGFFD-VLM outperforms existing methods in both text-based forgery judgment and analysis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Extended Dataset (DD-VQA+)**:  The creation of a more comprehensive and diverse deepfake VQA dataset (DD-VQA+) is a valuable contribution. The inclusion of face quality attributes is a sensible and practical extension, addressing a clear deficiency in previous datasets. The automated generation of some annotations is a plus.
*   **Attribute-Driven Hybrid LoRA Strategy**: The use of LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) for efficient fine-tuning of VLMs is not entirely new, but the **dynamic selection of LoRA experts based on image quality attributes is a novel and promising idea.** This allows the model to adapt its focus depending on the specific characteristics of the input image, which is particularly relevant for forgery detection where artifacts and quality vary significantly. This introduces a valuable adaptative approach.
*   **Multi-Granularity Prompt Learning**: The core idea of using multi-granularity prompts derived from forgery segmentation and classification is another contribution. This allows the model to focus on different levels of detail (coarse vs. fine-grained), improving both detection accuracy and interpretability. The prompt formulation itself can also be seen as a novel aspect.
*   **Forgery-Aware Training Strategy**: While staged training and specific loss functions are relatively common techniques in deep learning, their application to this specific problem and the careful design of the contrastive loss for image feature separation adds to the overall effectiveness of the framework.

**Significance:**

*   **Improved Accuracy**: The experimental results demonstrate that MGFFD-VLM outperforms existing methods, indicating a practical improvement in face forgery detection performance. The enhancements on top of Mobile-VLM are notable.
*   **Enhanced Interpretability**: The ability to generate human-understandable explanations for forgery detection is a major advantage, making the system more trustworthy and useful in real-world applications.
*   **Potential Impact**: The framework has the potential to advance the field of face forgery detection and contribute to the development of more robust and explainable AI systems. It tackles an important problem with increasing societal relevance.
*   **Weakness**: While the quantitative results are compelling, the qualitative examples (Figure 8) would benefit from more direct comparisons to the baseline MobileVLM, showing *specifically* what aspects MGFFD-VLM captures that MobileVLM misses. Also, while good, there could be more detailed exploration of why certain LoRA experts are selected for particular samples and how this impacts the final outcome. Furthermore, a more detailed ablation study to show exactly how each aspect of the loss functions effect the final performance.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign this paper a score of **7.5**.

*   **Rationale**: The paper presents a solid and well-designed framework for face forgery detection. The integration of multiple novel components, particularly the Attribute-Driven Hybrid LoRA strategy and Multi-Granularity Prompt Learning, makes it a significant contribution to the field. The experimental results are compelling, and the potential for enhanced interpretability is a major strength.

The main reasons preventing a higher score are:

*   It builds on existing methods and datasets.
*   The experimental comparisons could be more direct and insightful in showcasing the specific benefits of each proposed component, as highlighted in the "weakness" section above.
*   While the individual components are reasonably novel, the *combination* of these into a cohesive architecture could be seen as an incremental improvement rather than a complete paradigm shift.

Overall, the paper represents a strong and valuable contribution to the field of face forgery detection.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generate to Ground: Multimodal Text Conditioning Boosts Phrase Grounding in Medical Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12236v1)
- **Authors**: Felix Nützel, Mischa Dombrowski, Bernhard Kainz
- **Abstract**: Phrase grounding, i.e., mapping natural language phrases to specific image regions, holds significant potential for disease localization in medical imaging through clinical reports. While current state-of-the-art methods rely on discriminative, self-supervised contrastive models, we demonstrate that generative text-to-image diffusion models, leveraging cross-attention maps, can achieve superior zero-shot phrase grounding performance. Contrary to prior assumptions, we show that fine-tuning diffusion models with a frozen, domain-specific language model, such as CXR-BERT, substantially outperforms domain-agnostic counterparts. This setup achieves remarkable improvements, with mIoU scores doubling those of current discriminative methods. These findings highlight the underexplored potential of generative models for phrase grounding tasks. To further enhance performance, we introduce Bimodal Bias Merging (BBM), a novel post-processing technique that aligns text and image biases to identify regions of high certainty. BBM refines cross-attention maps, achieving even greater localization accuracy. Our results establish generative approaches as a more effective paradigm for phrase grounding in the medical imaging domain, paving the way for more robust and interpretable applications in clinical practice. The source code and model weights are available at https://github.com/Felix-012/generate_to_ground.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Generate to Ground: Multimodal Text Conditioning Boosts Phrase Grounding in Medical Vision-Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach to phrase grounding in medical images, particularly chest X-rays (CXRs).  Instead of relying on discriminative or self-supervised contrastive learning models (the dominant paradigm), the authors explore generative Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs). The core idea is to leverage cross-attention maps within the LDM to map phrases to image regions.  A key contribution is the replacement of the standard LDM text encoder with a domain-specific, pre-trained language model called CXR-BERT (specifically trained on CXR reports). They further introduce "Bimodal Bias Merging" (BBM), a post-processing technique to align text and image biases within the cross-attention maps, refining localization accuracy. The experiments demonstrate significant improvements in phrase grounding performance, measured by mIoU and Contrast-to-Noise Ratio (CNR), compared to existing methods, especially when using CXR-BERT and BBM.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several aspects:
    *   **Generative Approach:** Shifting from discriminative to generative models (LDMs) for phrase grounding in the medical domain is a significant departure and provides a fresh perspective.
    *   **Domain-Specific Text Conditioning:** The utilization of CXR-BERT as a frozen text encoder, rather than a generic encoder, is a well-motivated approach that leverages domain knowledge and leads to substantial performance gains.
    *   **Bimodal Bias Merging (BBM):** This post-processing technique is entirely new and aims to refine cross-attention maps and explicitly align biases from text and image modalities. It addresses a crucial alignment issue in VLMs.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper clearly shows that domain-specific VLMs outperform domain-agnostic models within the LDM framework.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in medical imaging: the ability to localize disease findings from textual reports without explicit labels. The improvements achieved are not incremental but substantial, with mIoU scores doubling those of current state-of-the-art discriminative methods. This is highly significant as it potentially enables more robust and interpretable applications of AI in clinical practice. The work sheds light on the underexplored potential of generative models for this task.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem statement:**  The motivation for interpretable phrase grounding in medical imaging is well-articulated.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper provides compelling quantitative evidence of the superiority of the proposed method.
    *   **Well-defined methodology:** The approach is clearly explained and easy to understand, along with justifications for each component (CXR-BERT, BBM).
    *   **Ablation studies:** The ablation study (Appendix A) provides valuable insights into the impact of different design choices and the contribution of each component.
    *   **Discussion of Limitations:** The paper is honest in acknowledging limitations (e.g., difficulties with Pneumothorax localization) and proposes directions for future research.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The release of code and model weights promotes reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **BBM Complexity:** While novel, the BBM technique could benefit from a more intuitive explanation and further simplification. The specific formulas, while technically sound, may be difficult for some readers to grasp initially.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper alludes to hyperparameter optimization, however, does not present how robust is the approach against changes in the parameters.
    *   **Limited Domain:**  While focused on CXRs, the generalizability of the approach to other medical imaging modalities or non-medical domains could be further discussed. The results may be specific to CXRs and the characteristics of CXR reports.
    *   **Comparison Baseline for LDMs:** The direct comparison with domain-specific BioViL is excellent; however, the authors could evaluate the impact of fine-tuning the entire LDM network and compare it with the current approach of freezing the text-encoder.

*   **Potential Impact:** This paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of medical image analysis. By demonstrating the effectiveness of generative models for phrase grounding, it opens up new avenues for research and development. The insights gained from this work can be applied to develop more reliable and interpretable AI tools for disease localization, report generation, and clinical decision support. It is likely to influence future research in multimodal medical AI.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a strong contribution with clear novelty and significant potential impact. While there are minor weaknesses in terms of explanation complexity and scope of validation, the strengths clearly outweigh these limitations. It offers a substantial performance boost over existing methods, introduces a novel bias merging technique, and leverages domain-specific knowledge effectively. The shift towards generative models in phrase grounding for medical imaging is a paradigm shift with considerable potential.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Improving Contextual ASR via Multi-grained Fusion with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12252v1)
- **Authors**: Shilin Zhou, Zhenghua Li
- **Abstract**: While end-to-end Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR) models have shown impressive performance in transcribing general speech, they often struggle to accurately recognize contextually relevant keywords, such as proper nouns or user-specific entities. Previous approaches have explored leveraging keyword dictionaries in the textual modality to improve keyword recognition, either through token-level fusion that guides token-by-token generation or phrase-level fusion that enables direct copying of keyword phrases. However, these methods operate at different granularities and have their own limitations. In this paper, we propose a novel multi-grained fusion approach that jointly leverages the strengths of both token-level and phrase-level fusion with Large Language Models (LLMs). Our approach incorporates a late-fusion strategy that elegantly combines ASR's acoustic information with LLM's rich contextual knowledge, balancing fine-grained token precision with holistic phrase-level understanding. Experiments on Chinese and English datasets demonstrate that our approach achieves state-of-the-art performance on keyword-related metrics while preserving high accuracy on non-keyword text. Ablation studies further confirm that the token-level and phrase-level components both contribute significantly to the performance gains, complementing each other in our joint multi-grained framework. The code and models will be publicly available at https://github.com/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of improving contextual Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR), specifically for keywords.  It proposes a multi-grained fusion approach that combines token-level and phrase-level fusion with Large Language Models (LLMs). Token-level fusion leverages the LLM to guide token generation, while phrase-level fusion enables direct copying of keyword phrases from a dictionary. The method employs a late-fusion strategy, balancing the precision of token-level information with the holistic understanding of phrase-level context. Experiments on Chinese and English datasets demonstrate improved performance on keyword-related metrics, maintaining accuracy on non-keyword text. Ablation studies confirm the contribution of both token-level and phrase-level components.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses an important problem:** Keyword recognition in ASR is a practical problem with real-world applications, and the paper targets it directly.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The multi-grained fusion approach is a well-motivated combination of existing techniques (token-level and phrase-level fusion) offering a more flexible and balanced approach. It moves beyond previous techniques that are limited to token-level operations or rely exclusively on speech information for phrase selection.
    *   **Thorough Experiments:** Experiments on multiple datasets (Chinese and English) and ablation studies provide evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method. The analysis of each module's impact is helpful.
    *   **Competitive Results:** The paper demonstrates performance comparable to or better than state-of-the-art methods, especially on keyword-related metrics.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and structured, with clear explanations of the proposed method and experimental setup.
    *   **LLM Incorporation:** The paper efficiently uses the LLM, mainly through prompting, which is less computationally heavy than full fine-tuning and direct LLM manipulation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the fusion is novel, the underlying techniques are well-established.  The contribution lies primarily in the integration, but the components themselves are not groundbreaking.
    *   **Complexity:** The approach is somewhat complex, involving multiple modules and fusion steps, raising potential concerns about deployment and efficiency in real-time applications. While RTF is reported, there is some increase in latency.
    *   **Dataset Dependence:** The performance improvement is closely tied to keyword density. Datasets without clearly defined keyword lists or with low keyword occurrence will likely see reduced gains.
    *   **LLM Size Dependency:** While it shows smaller LLMs are sufficient for this task, it hasn't extensively tested with larger models that may yield better results. Is there an upper limit on the LLM size?
    *   **Limited ablation experiments**: While they ablate phrase level and token level models, it could be useful to show experiments of simply using one LLM (e.g. qwen2-7B) instead of using two separate modules.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes to the ongoing research area of incorporating LLMs into ASR systems, particularly for specific tasks like keyword recognition.
    *   The multi-grained fusion approach offers a practical and effective solution that could be integrated into existing ASR pipelines.
    *   The experimental results provide valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of different fusion strategies.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed and valuable contribution to the field of contextual ASR. The multi-grained fusion approach is a logical extension of existing techniques, offering a more balanced and effective solution. The experiments are thorough and provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method. However, the incremental nature of the novelty, coupled with the complexity of the approach and its dependence on keyword density, limits its overall impact. While the results are competitive, they do not represent a significant leap forward in ASR performance. Thus a score of 6 reflects a solid contribution with notable strengths but also certain limitations regarding novelty and potential broader impact.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Next-Gen Museum Guides: Autonomous Navigation and Visitor Interaction with an Agentic Robot
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12273v1)
- **Authors**: Luca Garello, Francesca Cocchella, Alessandra Sciutti, Manuel Catalano, Francesco Rea
- **Abstract**: Autonomous robots are increasingly being tested into public spaces to enhance user experiences, particularly in cultural and educational settings. This paper presents the design, implementation, and evaluation of the autonomous museum guide robot Alter-Ego equipped with advanced navigation and interactive capabilities. The robot leverages state-of-the-art Large Language Models (LLMs) to provide real-time, context aware question-and-answer (Q&A) interactions, allowing visitors to engage in conversations about exhibits. It also employs robust simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM) techniques, enabling seamless navigation through museum spaces and route adaptation based on user requests. The system was tested in a real museum environment with 34 participants, combining qualitative analysis of visitor-robot conversations and quantitative analysis of pre and post interaction surveys. Results showed that the robot was generally well-received and contributed to an engaging museum experience, despite some limitations in comprehension and responsiveness. This study sheds light on HRI in cultural spaces, highlighting not only the potential of AI-driven robotics to support accessibility and knowledge acquisition, but also the current limitations and challenges of deploying such technologies in complex, real-world environments.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper and a critical evaluation, including a novelty and significance score with a detailed justification.

**Summary:**

This paper presents an autonomous museum guide robot, named Alter-Ego, that integrates large language model (LLM)-powered dialogue with advanced simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM) navigation. The robot is designed to provide personalized tours, answer questions about exhibits in real time, and adapt to user requests. The system was deployed and tested in a real museum setting with 34 participants. The study combines qualitative analysis of visitor-robot conversations and quantitative analysis of pre- and post-interaction surveys.  Results suggest the robot was generally well-received and contributed to an engaging museum experience, despite some limitations in comprehension and responsiveness. The paper explores human-robot interaction (HRI) in cultural spaces, highlighting the potential and limitations of AI-driven robotics for accessibility and knowledge acquisition.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Real-World Deployment:** The study's primary strength lies in its deployment in a genuine museum environment. This provides valuable insights into the practical challenges of implementing such systems, including dealing with dynamic environments, noise, and diverse user interactions.
    *   **Integration of LLM and SLAM:** The integration of LLMs for natural language understanding with SLAM for autonomous navigation is a significant contribution. The dynamic prompt engineering approach is particularly noteworthy as it tries to control the cost of using an LLM.
    *   **Mixed-Methods Approach:** The combination of qualitative (conversation analysis, feedback) and quantitative (surveys, metrics) data provides a comprehensive understanding of user experiences and system performance. The qualitative data is used to identify themes, enriching the quantitative findings.
    *   **Dynamic Prompt Engineering:** The use of a dynamic prompt to interrogate the LLM with a smaller number of tokens.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Core Components:** While the integration is valuable, the individual components (SLAM, LLMs) are not particularly novel. The paper relies on existing technologies, although the *specific* implementation within this context has value.
    *   **Comprehension and Responsiveness Issues:** The paper acknowledges limitations in the robot's comprehension and responsiveness. A deeper analysis of the specific causes of these failures would be beneficial, as well as how the dynamic prompt engineering has contributed to the failures. It would be beneficial to analyze how the robot deals with edge cases.
    *   **Limited Statistical Depth:** While quantitative data is presented, the statistical analysis is relatively basic. More advanced statistical methods could be used to explore the relationships between different variables and test the hypotheses more rigorously. The discussion of null results could also be expanded upon.
    *   **Small Sample Size:** Although 34 participants is reasonable, a larger sample would increase the statistical power and generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Limited Error Rate Analysis:** The paper acknowledges higher error rates (misunderstandings) in noisier environments. However, further exploration is needed regarding how the noise affects the models, and how these errors can be avoided.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes to the growing field of social robotics and HRI in cultural heritage settings. It demonstrates the potential of autonomous robots to enhance visitor experiences and provide personalized information.
    *   The study highlights the challenges of deploying such technologies in real-world environments, offering valuable lessons for future research and development. It also contributes to the field with real-world insights for museum deployments.
    *   The paper highlights a niche domain, where robots can provide tour guide-like assistance.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could influence the design and development of future museum guide robots.
    *   The findings could inform best practices for deploying social robots in public spaces.
    *   The study could inspire further research on the integration of LLMs and SLAM for autonomous navigation and interaction.
    *   The detailed qualitative analysis will provide insights for future work.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper provides a valuable contribution to the field through its real-world deployment and mixed-methods approach. However, the lack of significant novelty in the core components, the limitations in statistical depth, and the comprehension/responsiveness issues prevent it from achieving a higher score. The system works by integrating LLMs with SLAM in real-world settings, where the system is also designed to reduce the cost of using an LLM by dynamically prompting the LLM.

### FADE: Adversarial Concept Erasure in Flow Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12283v1)
- **Authors**: Zixuan Fu, Yan Ren, Finn Carter, Chenyue Wang, Ze Niu, Dacheng Yu, Emily Davis, Bo Zhang
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have demonstrated remarkable image generation capabilities, but also pose risks in privacy and fairness by memorizing sensitive concepts or perpetuating biases. We propose a novel \textbf{concept erasure} method for text-to-image diffusion models, designed to remove specified concepts (e.g., a private individual or a harmful stereotype) from the model's generative repertoire. Our method, termed \textbf{FADE} (Fair Adversarial Diffusion Erasure), combines a trajectory-aware fine-tuning strategy with an adversarial objective to ensure the concept is reliably removed while preserving overall model fidelity. Theoretically, we prove a formal guarantee that our approach minimizes the mutual information between the erased concept and the model's outputs, ensuring privacy and fairness. Empirically, we evaluate FADE on Stable Diffusion and FLUX, using benchmarks from prior work (e.g., object, celebrity, explicit content, and style erasure tasks from MACE). FADE achieves state-of-the-art concept removal performance, surpassing recent baselines like ESD, UCE, MACE, and ANT in terms of removal efficacy and image quality. Notably, FADE improves the harmonic mean of concept removal and fidelity by 5--10\% over the best prior method. We also conduct an ablation study to validate each component of FADE, confirming that our adversarial and trajectory-preserving objectives each contribute to its superior performance. Our work sets a new standard for safe and fair generative modeling by unlearning specified concepts without retraining from scratch.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper on FADE:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FADE (Fair Adversarial Diffusion Erasure), a novel method for removing specific concepts from text-to-image diffusion models without retraining from scratch. FADE combines a trajectory-aware fine-tuning strategy with an adversarial objective: it trains the diffusion model to "fool" a discriminator network that detects the presence of the target concept. The fine-tuning is constrained to salient parameters, and a trajectory preservation loss is used to maintain image fidelity. The authors provide theoretical guarantees that FADE minimizes mutual information between the erased concept and model outputs. Empirical evaluations on Stable Diffusion and FLUX, across object, celebrity, explicit content, and style erasure tasks, show that FADE achieves state-of-the-art performance compared to ESD, UCE, MACE, and ANT in terms of removal efficacy and image quality. Ablation studies validate the contribution of each component.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the **combination of adversarial training with trajectory preservation and salient parameter fine-tuning** for concept erasure in diffusion models.  Existing methods have used fine-tuning, weight editing, or trajectory steering, but the integration of an adversarial approach, explicitly targeting information removal and coupling it with a fairness-inspired attention to minimizing collateral damage, is a distinct contribution. The theoretical analysis providing guarantees of information minimization also adds to the novelty. In addition, extending this area to flow models offers more general applicability.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its addressing a critical challenge in generative modeling: the potential for privacy violations, biases, and misuse due to the models' ability to memorize sensitive information and generate harmful content. Removing unwanted concepts opens up avenues for safer and fairer generative AI.

    *   FADE's empirical success, outperforming existing methods, demonstrates the practical value of the proposed approach. It reduces concept leakage while preserving fidelity.
    *   The information-theoretic guarantee provides a stronger theoretical foundation for concept erasure, going beyond purely empirical demonstrations.
    *   The focus on fairness, addressing concerns about side effects on unrelated concepts, is particularly important.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The technical approach is well-motivated and clearly explained, combining multiple techniques (adversarial learning, trajectory preservation, salient weight fine-tuning) in a coherent framework.
    *   The paper provides theoretical justification for the method.
    *   The empirical evaluation is comprehensive, covering multiple models, datasets, and metrics, and comparing against a range of baselines.
    *   Ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each component.
    *   The results are strong, with FADE consistently outperforming existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper argues for improved efficiency compared to retraining from scratch, it does not offer a detailed runtime complexity analysis or a clear comparison of computational cost to other fine-tuning based methods, such as ANT. Although, runtimes are stated. A more thorough cost analysis would be beneficial.
    *   The limitations section, while acknowledging the need for the adversary to be able to "learn" the concept, could be expanded. Specifically, the paper mentions that highly out-of-distribution outputs might not be addressed. How can this weakness be further handled? This is a remaining practical risk and an avenue for future work that could be discussed in more detail.
    * While the 82% result of erasing 100 classes simultaneously is notable, it appears from the original paper that this result is from adding a simple 10-way classifier. Adding a 10-way classifier is common and may skew the results from the original research paper. The experiment should be re-done without the additional discriminator for the paper's novelty to be validated.
    * The research does not discuss the implications on fairness for multi-concept erasure. The team states that each concept requires a specific discriminator, but does not state the time implications or implications of erasing several classes. The team further specifies that each discriminator does not necessarily need to be trained fully. This may be impactful and should be analyzed as a risk of concept erasure.
    * A comparison of additional base models would be helpful for generalizing concept erasure.
    * The experiment, "Does machine unlearning truly remove model knowledge?" from Chen et al. (2025b) should be conducted for further validation.
    * The team did not provide a "Responsible Disclosure" as a risk to the research team, although that may be negligible, given that the model does not need to be trained from scratch.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   FADE has the potential to become a standard technique for concept erasure in diffusion models, particularly when both removal efficacy and fairness are important.
    *   The adversarial training framework and information-theoretic analysis could inspire new approaches to other problems in generative modeling, such as bias mitigation and privacy protection.
    *   The theoretical result connecting adversarial optimality to concept independence could lead to further theoretical work in this area.
    *   The method provides a practical approach that researchers and practitioners can implement immediately, contributing to safer and more responsible AI development.

**Score:** 8.5

**Rationale:**  The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of generative modeling by introducing a novel and effective method for concept erasure. The combination of adversarial learning with trajectory preservation, the information-theoretic guarantee, and the comprehensive empirical evaluation are all strong points. While there are limitations related to computational cost analysis and discussion of the model, these are minor compared to the overall contribution. The paper has the potential to have a lasting influence on the way concept erasure is performed in generative models and fosters greater focus on removing sensitive information. While additional comparison to base models would be helpful, the technique provides a compelling and novel method for more general application of concept removal techniques. Additional comparison to alternative generative models, would also provide more generally applicable conclusions. Furthermore, it is not validated without the single classifier model. These factors result in a score of 8.5, which recognizes the strengths of the paper while also acknowledging areas for future improvement.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Text-ADBench: Text Anomaly Detection Benchmark based on LLMs Embedding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12295v1)
- **Authors**: Feng Xiao, Jicong Fan
- **Abstract**: Text anomaly detection is a critical task in natural language processing (NLP), with applications spanning fraud detection, misinformation identification, spam detection and content moderation, etc. Despite significant advances in large language models (LLMs) and anomaly detection algorithms, the absence of standardized and comprehensive benchmarks for evaluating the existing anomaly detection methods on text data limits rigorous comparison and development of innovative approaches. This work performs a comprehensive empirical study and introduces a benchmark for text anomaly detection, leveraging embeddings from diverse pre-trained language models across a wide array of text datasets. Our work systematically evaluates the effectiveness of embedding-based text anomaly detection by incorporating (1) early language models (GloVe, BERT); (2) multiple LLMs (LLaMa-2, LLama-3, Mistral, OpenAI (small, ada, large)); (3) multi-domain text datasets (news, social media, scientific publications); (4) comprehensive evaluation metrics (AUROC, AUPRC). Our experiments reveal a critical empirical insight: embedding quality significantly governs anomaly detection efficacy, and deep learning-based approaches demonstrate no performance advantage over conventional shallow algorithms (e.g., KNN, Isolation Forest) when leveraging LLM-derived embeddings.In addition, we observe strongly low-rank characteristics in cross-model performance matrices, which enables an efficient strategy for rapid model evaluation (or embedding evaluation) and selection in practical applications. Furthermore, by open-sourcing our benchmark toolkit that includes all embeddings from different models and code at https://github.com/jicongfan/Text-Anomaly-Detection-Benchmark, this work provides a foundation for future research in robust and scalable text anomaly detection systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided paper abstract and the partial content to provide a summary and a rigorous evaluation.

**Summary**

The paper introduces Text-ADBench, a benchmark for text anomaly detection that leverages embeddings from various pre-trained language models (LLMs) across a diverse set of text datasets. The authors systematically evaluate the effectiveness of embedding-based text anomaly detection, incorporating early language models (GloVe, BERT), multiple LLMs (LLaMa-2, LLaMa-3, Mistral, OpenAI), multi-domain text datasets (news, social media, scientific publications), and comprehensive evaluation metrics (AUROC, AUPRC). The study reveals that embedding quality significantly impacts anomaly detection efficacy, and shallow algorithms (e.g., KNN, Isolation Forest) can perform competitively with deep learning approaches when using LLM-derived embeddings. The authors also observe low-rank characteristics in cross-model performance matrices, enabling efficient model evaluation and selection.  The benchmark toolkit, including embeddings and code, is publicly available to foster future research.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** While the general idea of using embeddings for anomaly detection is not entirely novel, this paper provides a more comprehensive benchmark, addressing limitations of prior works, such as Li et al., and Cao et al., as they mention in the paper. Specifically, it brings in multiple LLMs (including the most recent ones), multiple pooling strategies for text embedding aggregation, various traditional and specialized AD methods, comprehensive evaluation metrics (AUROC/AUPRC), and open-sourced embeddings and code. This significantly enhances the research landscape. The discovery of low-rank structures is also a valuable addition that can lead to efficient method selection. However, the detection algorithms themselves are not new; the innovation resides in the integration with diverse LLMs and a thorough comparison.
*   **Significance:** The absence of standardized benchmarks has been a bottleneck in text anomaly detection research. Text-ADBench addresses this directly, enabling more robust comparisons and facilitating the development of improved methods. The key empirical finding (that shallow algorithms can be surprisingly effective with LLM embeddings) is crucial and could reshape the focus of future research. The open-sourcing of the benchmark is also a significant contribution, as it promotes reproducibility and further development by other researchers.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive benchmark:** Text-ADBench includes a wide range of LLMs, text datasets, and anomaly detection algorithms, making it a valuable resource for researchers.
    *   **Empirical insights:** The paper provides critical insights into the performance of different embedding and anomaly detection techniques, which can guide future research efforts.
    *   **Open-source toolkit:** The availability of the benchmark toolkit promotes reproducibility and facilitates the development of new methods.
    *   **Identification of Low-Rank Structure:** This finding helps with an efficient method selection in the future, which makes it significantly valuable.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Exploration of Pooling Strategies:** While the paper does include different pooling strategies, it might benefit from a more in-depth investigation into why certain pooling strategies outperform others in specific scenarios.
    *   **Lack of Novel Algorithms:** The research relies on existing anomaly detection algorithms. While valuable as a benchmark, there aren't novel AD methods.

*   **Potential Influence:** Text-ADBench has the potential to become a widely used benchmark in the text anomaly detection community. This could lead to more rapid progress in the development of robust and scalable anomaly detection systems.

**Justification for the Score**

I am assigning a score of **8**. The paper constitutes a significant and practically valuable contribution to the field of text anomaly detection. Text-ADBench fills a critical gap by providing a comprehensive, open-source benchmark that addresses limitations of existing resources. The empirical insights and findings offer valuable guidance for future research directions. While the core anomaly detection algorithms are not novel, the systematic integration with LLMs, thorough comparison, and open-sourcing justify a high score. The few weaknesses present are minor and do not overshadow the overall strength and significance of the paper.

- **Score**: 8/10

### Humans are more gullible than LLMs in believing common psychological myths
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12296v1)
- **Authors**: Bevan Koopman, Guido Zuccon
- **Abstract**: Despite widespread debunking, many psychological myths remain deeply entrenched. This paper investigates whether Large Language Models (LLMs) mimic human behaviour of myth belief and explores methods to mitigate such tendencies. Using 50 popular psychological myths, we evaluate myth belief across multiple LLMs under different prompting strategies, including retrieval-augmented generation and swaying prompts. Results show that LLMs exhibit significantly lower myth belief rates than humans, though user prompting can influence responses. RAG proves effective in reducing myth belief and reveals latent debiasing potential within LLMs. Our findings contribute to the emerging field of Machine Psychology and highlight how cognitive science methods can inform the evaluation and development of LLM-based systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores whether Large Language Models (LLMs) exhibit human-like tendencies in believing common psychological myths, even though these myths are widely debunked. The study evaluates several LLMs using different prompting strategies, including neutral prompts, prompts designed to sway the LLM, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) prompts. The key findings are that LLMs generally demonstrate lower myth belief rates compared to humans, RAG proves to be an effective strategy for mitigating myth belief in LLMs, and that user prompting can significantly influence LLM responses, though RAG reduces this influence. The research contributes to the emerging field of Machine Psychology by using cognitive science methods to better understand and develop LLM-based systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Relevant and Timely Research:** The paper addresses a crucial issue regarding the potential for LLMs to perpetuate misinformation and align to human biases.
    *   **Sound Methodology:** The study employs a well-defined methodology, leveraging a standard dataset of psychological myths and systematically evaluating LLMs under various prompting conditions. The inclusion of RAG is a significant strength.
    *   **Clear Results and Analysis:** The results are presented clearly and analyzed in the context of the research questions. The visualizations (Figure 1) are effective in communicating the key findings.
    *   **Contribution to Machine Psychology:** The paper explicitly frames its work as a contribution to Machine Psychology, which strengthens its significance by linking it to a broader research agenda.
    *   **Consideration of mitigation strategies** The paper considers how to mitigate these beliefs.
    *   **Discussion of limitations** The paper takes the time to discuss some of the paper's limitations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Prompt Engineering:** The authors acknowledge limited prompt engineering. While different prompt types were used, a more comprehensive exploration could yield further insights. The paper could have benefitted from analysis on the quality of the RAG document.
    *   **Retrieval System limitations:** The paper notes that only one retrieval system was used. Other corpora, in particular specific and reputable corpora, may yield stronger conclusions.
    *   **General Purpose LLMs only:** The authors rightly point out that they only consider general purpose LLMs that have quality and training data and safety measures to reduce harmful responses. A follow up study could consider LLMs that are not general purpose and not safety guarded.
    *   **Lack of Qualitative Analysis:** While quantitative results are presented, a qualitative analysis of the LLM-generated explanations for their beliefs could offer a deeper understanding of the reasoning processes involved.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The paper's novelty lies in its systematic investigation of myth belief in LLMs, particularly the use of RAG to mitigate these beliefs and the analysis of the interplay between parameter and contextual knowledge.
    *   The findings are significant because they highlight the potential for LLMs to propagate misinformation and the importance of developing strategies to address this issue.
    *   The research contributes to the ongoing debate about the ethical and societal implications of LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper presents a valuable and well-executed study on a relevant topic. The methodology is sound, and the results are clearly presented. The inclusion of RAG as a mitigation strategy is a notable strength. While there are some limitations (prompt engineering, limited LLM types), the paper offers significant insights into the behavior of LLMs in relation to common psychological myths. The study contributes meaningfully to the emerging field of Machine Psychology and highlights the importance of considering the potential for LLMs to perpetuate misinformation. Overall, this paper has a good methodology, novel ideas, and clear results with room for further exploration. Therefore, I assess this paper's quality as:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Chain-of-Descriptions: Improving Code LLMs for VHDL Code Generation and Summarization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12308v1)
- **Authors**: Prashanth Vijayaraghavan, Apoorva Nitsure, Charles Mackin, Luyao Shi, Stefano Ambrogio, Arvind Haran, Viresh Paruthi, Ali Elzein, Dan Coops, David Beymer, Tyler Baldwin, Ehsan Degan
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have become widely used across diverse NLP tasks and domains, demonstrating their adaptability and effectiveness. In the realm of Electronic Design Automation (EDA), LLMs show promise for tasks like Register-Transfer Level (RTL) code generation and summarization. However, despite the proliferation of LLMs for general code-related tasks, there's a dearth of research focused on evaluating and refining these models for hardware description languages (HDLs), notably VHDL. In this study, we evaluate the performance of existing code LLMs for VHDL code generation and summarization using various metrics and two datasets -- VHDL-Eval and VHDL-Xform. The latter, an in-house dataset, aims to gauge LLMs' understanding of functionally equivalent code. Our findings reveal consistent underperformance of these models across different metrics, underscoring a significant gap in their suitability for this domain. To address this challenge, we propose Chain-of-Descriptions (CoDes), a novel approach to enhance the performance of LLMs for VHDL code generation and summarization tasks. CoDes involves generating a series of intermediate descriptive steps based on: (i) the problem statement for code generation, and (ii) the VHDL code for summarization. These steps are then integrated with the original input prompt (problem statement or code) and provided as input to the LLMs to generate the final output. Our experiments demonstrate that the CoDes approach significantly surpasses the standard prompting strategy across various metrics on both datasets. This method not only improves the quality of VHDL code generation and summarization but also serves as a framework for future research aimed at enhancing code LLMs for VHDL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Chain-of-Descriptions: Improving Code LLMs for VHDL Code Generation and Summarization" explores the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) for VHDL (Very High-Speed Integrated Circuit Hardware Description Language) code generation and summarization, a relatively under-explored area compared to languages like Python.  The authors found that existing LLMs underperform on VHDL tasks. To address this, they propose a novel "Chain-of-Descriptions" (CoDes) framework. CoDes involves generating intermediate descriptive steps based on the problem statement (for code generation) or VHDL code (for summarization).  These descriptions are then incorporated into the LLM's input prompt to guide the generation of more accurate and coherent outputs. They evaluate their framework on VHDL-Eval and a new in-house dataset, VHDL-Xform (containing functionally equivalent code clones), demonstrating significant performance improvements over standard prompting strategies.  The paper analyzes the effectiveness of different aspects of CoDes, including the impact of prompt length, planning strategies (AST-based vs. line-based for summarization), and execution strategies (single-step vs. multi-step).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the application of a "chain-of-thought"-like approach, "CoDes," specifically tailored to VHDL code generation and summarization. While chain-of-thought prompting is not entirely new, its adaptation and evaluation in the context of a hardware description language like VHDL are a valuable contribution.  The creation of VHDL-Xform dataset is also a notable contribution, as it addresses the lack of resources for evaluating LLMs in the VHDL domain with a focus on functional equivalence. The AST-based planning strategy although not consistently outperforming line-by-line explanations, offers a fresh perspective on code summarization.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it tackles a gap in research concerning LLMs for hardware description languages.  Automating VHDL code generation and summarization can have a practical impact on Electronic Design Automation (EDA), reducing manual coding effort and improving understanding of complex circuits. By demonstrating the limitations of general-purpose code LLMs on VHDL and providing a method to improve their performance, the paper sets a baseline and provides a framework for future research. The analysis of different aspects of the CoDes framework (prompt length, planning, execution) gives insights into what works best.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the problem, the proposed solution, and the experimental setup.
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the CoDes framework.
    *   The inclusion of VHDL-Xform provides a valuable resource for future research.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each element of CoDes.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The VHDL-Xform dataset, while a contribution, could be further expanded and diversified to cover a broader range of VHDL code constructs and complexity levels.  The transformations, while well-defined, might benefit from more sophisticated techniques to create more challenging, yet functionally equivalent, code.
    *   The evaluation, while comprehensive, primarily relies on automated metrics (Pass@k, ROUGE-L) and LLM preference rate.  A more in-depth qualitative analysis of the generated VHDL code and summaries, perhaps involving expert VHDL designers, would strengthen the validation.
    *   The single-sampling runs, may not provide a full characterisation of performance.
    *   While CoDes shows improvements, the absolute performance numbers (e.g., Pass@1 scores in the 20-25% range) indicate that there's still considerable room for improvement. This suggests that CoDes might not fully address the challenges of VHDL synthesis and abstraction.
    *   The reliance on LLM preference rate as an evaluation metric can introduce bias. This is partially mitigated by selecting a powerful LLM, but the choice of judge model will always introduce bias.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper will likely influence future research on LLMs for hardware description languages.  It highlights the need for specialized approaches tailored to VHDL and provides a starting point for developing more effective techniques. The VHDL-Xform dataset could become a benchmark for evaluating future models. Also, it highlights that general-purpose techniques like CoT do not necessarily work well out-of-the-box, and require specialized tuning for specific domains and tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach to improve LLM performance on VHDL code generation and summarization, backed by solid experimental results and a valuable dataset contribution. However, the limitations of the dataset, evaluation methods, and the absolute performance numbers prevent it from being considered a truly groundbreaking contribution. More sophisticated and extensive evaluation, improved datasets, and a deeper dive into advanced VHDL constructs and abstraction complexities are desirable in future iterations. Therefore, considering all factors, the paper earns a score that acknowledges its contribution, but emphasizes room for improvement.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Thought Purity: Defense Paradigm For Chain-of-Thought Attack
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12314v1)
- **Authors**: Zihao Xue, Zhen Bi, Long Ma, Zhenlin Hu, Yan Wang, Zhenfang Liu, Qing Sheng, Jie Xiao, Jungang Lou
- **Abstract**: While reinforcement learning-trained Large Reasoning Models (LRMs, e.g., Deepseek-R1) demonstrate advanced reasoning capabilities in the evolving Large Language Models (LLMs) domain, their susceptibility to security threats remains a critical vulnerability. This weakness is particularly evident in Chain-of-Thought (CoT) generation processes, where adversarial methods like backdoor prompt attacks can systematically subvert the model's core reasoning mechanisms. The emerging Chain-of-Thought Attack (CoTA) reveals this vulnerability through exploiting prompt controllability, simultaneously degrading both CoT safety and task performance with low-cost interventions. To address this compounded security-performance vulnerability, we propose Thought Purity (TP): a defense paradigm that systematically strengthens resistance to malicious content while preserving operational efficacy. Our solution achieves this through three synergistic components: (1) a safety-optimized data processing pipeline (2) reinforcement learning-enhanced rule constraints (3) adaptive monitoring metrics. Our approach establishes the first comprehensive defense mechanism against CoTA vulnerabilities in reinforcement learning-aligned reasoning systems, significantly advancing the security-functionality equilibrium for next-generation AI architectures.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Thought Purity: Defense Paradigm For Chain-of-Thought Attack":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the vulnerability of Reinforcement Learning-trained Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) to Chain-of-Thought Attack (CoTA).  CoTA exploits prompt controllability to subvert reasoning processes. The paper proposes a defense paradigm called Thought Purity (TP), which aims to strengthen resistance to malicious content while preserving operational efficacy.  TP comprises a safety-optimized data processing pipeline, Reinforcement Learning-enhanced rule constraints via a modified GRPO algorithm, and adaptive monitoring metrics. The authors claim TP is the first comprehensive defense against CoTA in RL-aligned reasoning systems, promising better security-functionality balance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the formulation of the Thought Purity (TP) paradigm specifically tailored for defending against CoTA in LRMs, *not* just LLMs generally.  While individual components like data processing pipelines and RL-based defenses have been explored before, their *synergistic combination* targeting the unique vulnerabilities of LRMs is relatively new. The adaptation of GRPO algorithm for the purpose of defending against the attacks is a contribution, though based on an existing algorithm.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing importance of LRMs and their demonstrated vulnerability to prompt injection attacks.  CoTA's ability to degrade both CoT safety and task performance is a concerning vulnerability.  A comprehensive defense mechanism like TP has the potential to improve the reliability and trustworthiness of these systems. However, the paper's significance hinges on the *demonstrated effectiveness* of TP in real-world scenarios and its scalability to larger, more complex models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Targeted Problem:** The paper tackles a specific and timely security vulnerability in LRMs.
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** TP offers a multi-faceted approach combining data processing, RL, and monitoring.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper outlines the methodology, data processing, and the GRPO adaptation.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The paper presents results on multiple datasets and models, supporting the effectiveness of TP.
    *   **Metrics:** The novel metrics (Cure Rate and Reject Rate) provides a way to measure performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While the synergistic combination is novel, individual components are not entirely groundbreaking.  The value is in how they are brought together.
    *   **Data Dependency:** The data processing pipeline, particularly synthetic CoT generation and label implantation, relies heavily on the quality of GPT-4o. Its effectiveness may vary with prompt/input complexity.
    *   **Generality Concerns:** The experiments are conducted on specific datasets. The generalizability of TP to other LRMs, attack vectors, and reasoning tasks needs further investigation.
    *   **Anti-TP results are not fully explored:** The results that reverse the TP process should be looked at to discover new kinds of safety concerns.
    *   **OutputRL is insufficient baseline:** Should include more modern ways of solving the problem.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in LRM security by:
    *   Providing a framework (TP) for designing defenses against prompt injection attacks.
    *   Motivating further exploration of RL-based defenses for reasoning systems.
    *   Highlighting the importance of balancing security and performance in LRMs.
    *   Inspiring the development of more robust data processing pipelines and monitoring metrics.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution but has some limitations. While it presents a well-defined approach to defend against a specific attack on LRMs, the novelty is somewhat incremental, and the generalizability needs more validation. The experimental results look promising, and the problem is very relevant, but more modern baselines are needed to compare against. Also, it needs further experiments on different types of LLMs.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Compositional Discrete Latent Code for High Fidelity, Productive Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12318v1)
- **Authors**: Samuel Lavoie, Michael Noukhovitch, Aaron Courville
- **Abstract**: We argue that diffusion models' success in modeling complex distributions is, for the most part, coming from their input conditioning. This paper investigates the representation used to condition diffusion models from the perspective that ideal representations should improve sample fidelity, be easy to generate, and be compositional to allow out-of-training samples generation. We introduce Discrete Latent Code (DLC), an image representation derived from Simplicial Embeddings trained with a self-supervised learning objective. DLCs are sequences of discrete tokens, as opposed to the standard continuous image embeddings. They are easy to generate and their compositionality enables sampling of novel images beyond the training distribution. Diffusion models trained with DLCs have improved generation fidelity, establishing a new state-of-the-art for unconditional image generation on ImageNet. Additionally, we show that composing DLCs allows the image generator to produce out-of-distribution samples that coherently combine the semantics of images in diverse ways. Finally, we showcase how DLCs can enable text-to-image generation by leveraging large-scale pretrained language models. We efficiently finetune a text diffusion language model to generate DLCs that produce novel samples outside of the image generator training distribution.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Discrete Latent Codes (DLCs), a novel image representation derived from Simplicial Embeddings (SEMs) trained with a self-supervised learning objective.  DLCs consist of sequences of discrete tokens designed to improve the fidelity, ease of generation, and compositionality of diffusion models.  The authors demonstrate that diffusion models trained with DLCs achieve improved generation fidelity, setting a new state-of-the-art for unconditional ImageNet image generation. The paper also shows how DLCs enable out-of-distribution (OOD) sample generation by composing DLCs from different images and text-to-image generation using large-scale pre-trained language models to generate DLCs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the use of discrete latent codes derived from SEMs to condition diffusion models.  While discrete latent spaces have been explored previously in the context of generative models (e.g., VQ-VAE), the application to conditioning diffusion models in this specific way, using SEMs, and demonstrating significant improvements in fidelity and compositionality is a valuable contribution.  The compositionality aspect, enabling OOD generation, and the integration with large language models for text-to-image synthesis further enhance the novelty. The remasking strategy to improve generation quality for SEDD-Absorb is a minor contribution.

*   **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from its potential to address key limitations of existing diffusion models, such as difficulty in modeling highly multimodal distributions and generating novel OOD samples.  Achieving state-of-the-art FID for unconditional ImageNet generation is a strong empirical result demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The ability to perform semantic composition by mixing and matching DLCs from different images opens up exciting possibilities for creative image generation and control.  The text-to-image framework leverages powerful pre-trained LLMs, reducing the need for vast labeled image-text datasets, making the method more accessible. While the experimental results are compelling, there are some aspects where it could be improved:

    *   **Ablation Study:** It would be good to better understand the relative contributions of SEM training objective and discrete latent space. Is the improvement primarily due to SEM or is it simply having a discrete code which is more important?

    *   **Scalability:** While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of the approach on ImageNet, it is unclear how well it would scale to more complex and diverse datasets. The computational cost and memory requirements of training SEMs and diffusion models with DLCs should also be discussed in more detail.

    *   **Qualitative Results:** The qualitative results on composition and text-to-image generation are promising, but more comprehensive user studies would be needed to assess the perceptual quality and coherence of the generated images.

    *   **Comparison:** More direct comparison with alternative techniques for conditional generation, such as approaches based on contrastive learning or adversarial training, would provide a more complete picture of the strengths and weaknesses of DLCs.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow. The figures and tables are informative.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of generative modeling by providing a more effective and versatile approach for conditioning diffusion models.  The compositional capabilities and text-to-image framework could inspire new research directions in creative content generation and controllable image synthesis. However, its limited scalability would hinder wide spread adoption.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and technically sound approach for conditioning diffusion models using DLCs derived from SEMs. The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements in generation fidelity and enable new capabilities for compositional image synthesis and text-to-image generation. While the paper has some limitations in terms of scalability and detailed analysis, its overall contribution is significant, warranting a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unsupervised Monocular 3D Keypoint Discovery from Multi-View Diffusion Priors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12336v1)
- **Authors**: Subin Jeon, In Cho, Junyoung Hong, Seon Joo Kim
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces KeyDiff3D, a framework for unsupervised monocular 3D keypoints estimation that accurately predicts 3D keypoints from a single image. While previous methods rely on manual annotations or calibrated multi-view images, both of which are expensive to collect, our method enables monocular 3D keypoints estimation using only a collection of single-view images. To achieve this, we leverage powerful geometric priors embedded in a pretrained multi-view diffusion model. In our framework, this model generates multi-view images from a single image, serving as a supervision signal to provide 3D geometric cues to our model. We also use the diffusion model as a powerful 2D multi-view feature extractor and construct 3D feature volumes from its intermediate representations. This transforms implicit 3D priors learned by the diffusion model into explicit 3D features. Beyond accurate keypoints estimation, we further introduce a pipeline that enables manipulation of 3D objects generated by the diffusion model. Experimental results on diverse aspects and datasets, including Human3.6M, Stanford Dogs, and several in-the-wild and out-of-domain datasets, highlight the effectiveness of our method in terms of accuracy, generalization, and its ability to enable manipulation of 3D objects generated by the diffusion model from a single image.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KeyDiff3D, a novel framework for unsupervised monocular 3D keypoint estimation. The core idea is to leverage geometric priors embedded within a pre-trained multi-view diffusion model. The framework generates multi-view images from a single input image using the diffusion model, which act as a supervision signal to guide 3D keypoint learning without requiring manual annotations, camera parameters, or multi-view images during training. KeyDiff3D also uses the diffusion model as a feature extractor, constructing 3D feature volumes from intermediate representations. The method further includes a pipeline for manipulating 3D objects generated by the diffusion model, using the estimated keypoints and connectivity graphs to create animatable models. The approach is evaluated on human and animal datasets, demonstrating accuracy, generalization capabilities to in-the-wild and out-of-domain scenarios, and object manipulation abilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its clever use of pre-trained multi-view diffusion models as a source of geometric priors for unsupervised monocular 3D keypoint estimation. While prior works have explored unsupervised 3D keypoint learning and the use of diffusion models in visual understanding, the combination of these elements to address the challenging task of monocular 3D keypoint estimation is original. The approach of constructing 3D feature volumes from the diffusion model's internal representations is also a significant contribution, transforming implicit priors into explicit 3D features. The manipulation pipeline that creates animatable objects is a nice addition showcasing the 3D understanding learned.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work on the field is considerable. Removing the need for both 3D annotations *and* calibrated multi-view images significantly lowers the barrier for learning 3D object representations, enabling applications in scenarios where collecting such data is difficult or impossible. The demonstrated generalization capabilities further increase its real-world applicability. The creation of animatable 3D objects opens up interesting avenues for downstream tasks like animation and content creation. The method is object-agnostic, showcasing generalizability to various objects, including humans and animals.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The idea of extracting geometric priors from diffusion models for unsupervised 3D learning is well-motivated and elegantly executed.
    *   The method demonstrates good performance on various datasets, including in-the-wild images, indicating its practical potential.
    *   The inclusion of a pipeline for 3D object manipulation adds value and showcases the capabilities of the learned representations.
    *   The paper is clearly written and presents a thorough evaluation.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The performance is dependent on the quality of the pre-trained diffusion model. Limitations of the diffusion model (e.g., difficulty in handling unusual poses or objects) will directly impact KeyDiff3D's performance. While this dependency is explicitly mentioned, it's a critical limitation that could hinder its performance on certain complex real-world scenarios.
    *   The computational cost of extracting features from the diffusion model could be a bottleneck in some applications. The authors acknowledge the multiple denoising steps as a source of higher computational cost.
    *   The diffusion model focuses on masked image regions, ignoring potentially useful scene context.
*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a clever and well-executed approach to a challenging problem, unsupervised monocular 3D keypoint estimation, which is a crucial task for a range of applications. The key contribution is the creative extraction and utilization of 3D geometric priors learned by multi-view diffusion models. While the method has limitations related to the dependence on diffusion model quality and higher computational cost, its strengths, including strong results and good generalizability, outweigh its weaknesses. This work offers significant progress towards scalable 3D understanding.
